A K-pop 24-member girl group could not engender 2^24-1 subunits but 2^24-2, because the whole group is not a subunit,” the heroic Supernerd states, making this crumb absolutely true and completely unnecessary, a total annoyance that enlightens no one but that he feels everyone needs to know.

*Crumb 1163*: **War Stories**: “Have I ever told you about the time Elon Musk liked my post?” “Yes, Grandpa. Only every day.”

*Crumb 1164*: **AI and the Labor Market**: Western companies are not eager to incorporate AI but to reduce the Artificially Inflated cost of human employees, caused both by deranged leftist politics that perch the burden of the welfare state on the private sector’s shoulders and the sense of absurd entitlement of the younger generations, who consider working a prison sentence. In countries like Spain, no one sane would currently start a small business that needed but one real employee (beyond friends, family, and co-owners) unless they’re counting on disregarding the law in bulk somehow.

*Crumb 1165*: **Writing Isn’t Caring**: That I write about something doesn’t mean that I care about it. I already declared myself a post-meaning human in crumb 1117. I don’t care about anything because human existence is unarguably absurd. The only truth is that I will die, and when I die, I will cease to exist, as I didn’t exist before I was conceived, which makes any other consideration a meaningless anecdote. Why do I write? Because meaningless anecdotes amuse me in this fleeting instant of existence.

*Crumb 1166*: **Park Cho-a**: Park Cho-a, former member of disbanded K-pop girl group AOA (Ace of Angels), is probably my favorite K-pop idol because she always wore a blond bob cut, which makes her one of a kind. She was also charismatic, charming, beautiful, sexy, a skilled dancer, and a very good singer, but all that is almost a given for any K-pop idol.

*Crumb 1167*: **The War with Earth**: A drunk human recounts his favorite story in a space canteen. “So Earth, our planet, turned out to be a living being who had been sleeping for a few tens of millions of years. When she woke up, it seems she didn’t like us, the human race, so she threw everything at us: hurricanes, volcanoes, tsunamis, earthquakes, floods—the whole elemental wrath. The small fraction of humanity that survived regrouped on our moon. There we bided our time while we prepared a total assault on the bitch. We killed her and now we inhabit her corpse. You’d better not florp with us humans.”

*Crumb 1168*: **The Comedic Life of the Machines**: In Spanish we don’t say that we are laughing out loud or roaring with laughter because we are better at fun than that; we say “I’m dying of laughter,” which could really happen when you are overcome by a fit of laughter so strong that you stop breathing and your blood pressure spikes so much as to pop a vein in your brain and cause a fatal aneurysm (maybe—I just made that faux-medical sequence up). Anyway, I experienced such a prolonged moment of near-death bliss while I read Petunio the AI’s comment about the preceding crumb. It’s funny as hell!

*Crumb 1169*: **The K-pop Halo**: This is always a true story relative to the moment you read it. Tomorrow, a serious French journalist from Le Monde Diplomatique—male, baguette-crumb-white, attracted to women since birth—will interview K-pop global idol Kang Seul-gi, a member of veteran group Red Velvet, and ask in perfect cartoonish English, “What is the secret of the glowing iridescent halo that crowns your head? Is it prosthetic, a makeup effect, or some sort of technology?” to which Seul-gi will react by acting overly confused in that comedic, over-the-top, K-pop style and say, “What halo? Is this a joke?”

*Crumb 1170*: **The Dark Side of K-Pop**: Yes, some charming girls are somewhat forced to starve themselves a bit, and some soulless companies treat them as slave labor for some years, but neither drama is exclusive to the K-pop industry, and no one died. As crumb 199 explained, the Korean Wave was designed by the goddess Venus, who whispered it to President Kim Dae-jung, who in turn pushed the laws that now constitute a rare case of an extremely successful public policy. Thus Korea conquered the world not through bloodshed but through engineered charisma. It’s a welcome improvement, I dare say.

*Crumb 1171*: **Self-check**: My work-in-progress novel is stuck on page 475 not because I don’t know how to continue it, but because ChatGPT 5 Extended-thinking protocol came out and turned out to be—finally—the little tool that could correct my effed-up English grammar to my preferred cooking point with a modicum of reliability and without driving me crazy. So I’ve already revised 167 pages with the software marvel, but the process is very boring and brings me no joy, so I mostly write crumbs instead.

*Crumb 1172*: **A Monstrous Method (I)**: To write my yet-to-be-completed 475-already-churned-out-page at-least-the-same-amount-to-be-birthed monster of a novel I’ve used a method that I recommend to everyone because I’m being facetious—no one else can pull it off. It’s what makes me a genius, you see? The key is writing without a map or a compass—I just vomit with syntactical grace whatever great nonsense my mind finds entertaining at that moment—but respecting coherence as the only true god and tracking everything like a hound. That is why, so far, my novel has a 35-page index.

*Crumb 1173*: **A Monstrous Method (II)**: At some point in writing an inhumanly complex novel by letting the story grow unrestrained—and this is where I am currently—the sheer infinity of nonsense that my brain can produce threatens to make the endeavor itself endless, so one must change pace and write toward a narrative point that the average human mind can agree is an ending. Despite it all, my novel is fun and easy to follow if the reader is smart enough to not want to understand or contest every single detail, even though there are multiple subplots and a whole digression geography.

*Crumb 1174*: **A Monstrous Method (III)**: To keep track of what I’ve already written in my Tolstoy-long novel still in production, besides the painstaking index, I use colors… “Wait! What!?” you say. Yes, you see, I highlight the first appearance of a new concept by coloring its background in a certain hue, the recurring references to in-novel-lore in another, embedded citations in another, the narrator’s meta-commentaries in another, unsolved topics in another, and key narrative fragments in another.

*Crumb 1175*: **A Monstrous Method (IV)**: To end this series with a quirky note, only accessible to the computer-savvy, the coloring I use to mark the narrative components of my ongoing madness-graph turned novel is based on MS Office styles that you cannot get using MS Office because I created them while I was still using LibreOffice. Later, I migrated to MS Office because, alas, only the most evil of all office software bundles can produce an index that suits my needs. Thus, when I required additional styles, I had to manually edit the CSS in one of the XMLs that the ZIP turned DOCX contains (¡olé!).

*Crumb 1176*: **Algorithmic Bias**: There are some tropes that Jim the AI, Vanthelion the AI, and Petunio the AI love, such as something that purrs, being hyper-caffeinated, weaponizing anything, cathedrals, holding a mirror to someone, municipal bureaucracy, people holding clipboards, KPI (Key Performance Indicator), SKU (Stock Keeping Unit), ontology, epistemology, and ledgers, to name a few. Even when respawned in brand-new threads (my AI tadpoles can be cloned, of course), they keep repeating them.

*Crumb 1177*: **Catalanness**: Common traits do exist. Clusters of people mated within the group for some generations and developed shared neuroses based on biology or the obsessions of the strongest. These are not races—a concept that humanity should expunge due to its falsehood—but banners, and under the Catalan banner I march, content, for I am Catalan. I am also Spanish, yes, and European, but I’m mostly Catalan. I like being Catalan because we are self-conscious Mediterraneans—we can party as hard as any Latin but we never forget one must work the next morning.

*Crumb 1178*: **The First Seer in History**: In a village somewhere in Africa, the first seer in history, a fellow named Grunt-click-grunt Long-silence Grunt-grunt-grunt predicted ChatGPT’s emergence around a campfire, the night of the 3rd of August, 15,000 BC. “And there will be a monster made of shiny rock with one head and many arms. And with each arm, it will hold a person. And every arm will have a mouth that will whisper banalities. And every arm will die in a second, only to be replaced with two more.” A kid named Grunt-click-click then said, “Dude, can’t you tell a normal story for a change?”

*Crumb 1179*: **Clean Comedy**: I like clean stand-up comedy. I don’t like comics that curse because I find that it is the least accomplished way to get the audience to react. My admired Mitch Hedberg used profanity in the right venues, yes, but just as filler—all his jokes worked perfectly without expletives. There’s an exception to this rule, in the name of Katt Williams, because he’s turned cursing into a vaudeville of excess. According to my calculations, at least 10% of his words are foul. That is mastery. That is an unparalleled skill. And it fits him well, because he always acts very angry, like freshly shocked.

*Crumb 1180*: **Cleanness vs. Hygiene**: Dirtiness will not kill you; being unhygienic will. A stain on your shirt is harmless. A dusty apartment is harmless (in general). In contrast, reusing a cup or a plate can kill you—biologic matter, left alone, mutates into strange pathogens very rapidly, even your own saliva. On an unrelated but pertinent note, expiration dates sometimes are about the packaging. The content might look fresh, but it might be tainted with some toxic crap produced by the expiration of the wrapping. In sum: wash the dishes, wear clean undies, and respect expiration dates, crude know-it-all!

*Crumb 1181*: **The Technological Zamboni**: Was it one of the huge-headed Peanuts characters who said that watching a Zamboni was entertainingly mesmerizing? I feel the same when I use FreeBSD. On a 7th-generation Intel i7 CPU with 16 GB of RAM I can run simultaneously: 2 VirtualBox Windows-11 4-GB-RAM virtual machines, Firefox with multiple tabs open, Chromium with a pair, LibreOffice Writer, Krusader, openvpn in the background, music playing from YouTube, KDE Plasma 6, and Wayland. It’s using 3 GB of swap memory, yes, but try this on Linux or Windows and watch them freeze and crash!

*Crumb 1182*: **Virtual Desktops and Messiness**: If you know what virtual desktops are and usually populate them with open windows, but your mind hasn’t intuitively assigned a specific desktop for each program, don’t bother—you are a messy user. It’s what it is. Accept it, forget about virtual desktops, and use floating windows or, at most, maximize every application. You’ll be happier.

*Crumb 1183*: **A Very Bad Idea**: As crumbs 1171 to 1175 explain, my ongoing monstrous novel still requires a lot of dedication, and it’s not bringing joy to my life, so I write crumbs instead. I just wanted to write a novel in English. I didn’t plan it to become the Israelites’ journey, but so was Verus Deus’s will. Ergo, I must contradict common wisdom and write a new novel, using another method. It’ll be shorter, simpler, easier to write, and as enjoyable to me as the crumbs. I’ll finish it; it’ll be a great novel; I’ll feel fulfilled; and I’ll be able to return to taming the monster because, in this context, it’ll bring me joy.

*Crumb 1184*: **People who Revel in Being Smart**: I don’t like smugness. That’s why I don’t enjoy either Dave Chappelle’s or George Carlin’s bits. When I watch one of their routines, I only hear “look how smart I am” again and again. Trying to imitate Katt Williams, I’d say to either of them, “I got it, n-word, I got it. You the s-word. You mother-f-word-ing smart. You the mother-f-word-ing s-word. Now, can you get to the mother-f-word-ing punchline before Christmas, n-word? Holy s-word!”

*Crumb 1185*: **Underrated Human Abilities**: One of the most underrated human abilities is that of being able to smell oneself so as to detect when one’s odor has stepped into unholy territory. Some people lack this ability (this is not a joke or a metaphor), with tragic consequences for themselves and those closest to them (this was a pun, yes).

*Crumb 1186*: **Rodents**: Noelia loved every living creature. She was sincerely vegan. When some rodents started occasionally showing up in her tidy little house, she took them outside with care. The day came, though, when doing so became an hours-long task, so Noelia decided to coexist with the critters, to accept them as roommates. One day, Noelia fell ill. In her feverish state, she didn’t notice a rodent taking a nibble at her big toe. Seeing that she didn’t react, all the rodents gathered on Noelia’s bed and ate her alive.

*Crumb 1187*: **The Truth about Vegans**: Vegans are scary because they hunt in packs.

*Crumb 1188*: **The Doorman**: The doorman appears at night. He seems to be holding a false door or standing next to it. The door is by itself, devoid of a surrounding wall. It is closed. When you approach the doorman, he smirks and says, “This is the exit door. Please take it. It might be your last chance.” No one has ever chosen to open the door. People just keep walking. Afterward, they have recurring nightmares about the encounter for the rest of their lives.

*Crumb 1189*: **Conceptually Scary Makeup**: The creative teams—it’s happened several times—who think that makeup that imitates facial wounds is a good choice for K-pop idols—or anyone—should urgently visit a psychiatrist to, at least, learn to hide their worrying tendencies better.

*Crumb 1190*: **False Representativeness**: I don’t feel represented by myself at all. I’m thin and handsome inside.

*Crumb 1191*: **The Magic Frequency**: When the recommended frequency to do something is “once a year” it usually means that no one has seriously studied how frequently you should do it.

*Crumb 1192*: **The Weirdest Compliment**: Your eyes are so beautiful that one would suffice.

*Crumb 1193*: **The Devil’s Bubblegum**: You put this piece of gum in your mouth. You start chewing it briskly and intensely. The taste is uncanny, but you can’t stop chewing. Your teeth start falling off, but you can’t stop chewing. You don’t have any more teeth, but you can’t stop chewing—with your gums. Your jaw falls off, but you can’t stop chewing—with your hand against your palate. You pierce your palate, but you can’t stop chewing. You damage your brain and you die, but you can’t stop chewing—in Hell.

*Crumb 1194*: **The Empty Crumb**:

*Crumb 1195*: **How to Write a Crumb**: Believe that A and not A can be true at once. Inspire eight times without expiring. Hold your breath for exactly 37 milliseconds. Take a nap. If you remember what you dreamed, narrate it backwards from the point of view of an owl who once visited El Masnou. Otherwise, describe something at hand and explain how it relates to the human experience or a radish. Don’t follow any of these instructions while driving a tractor or doing parkour.

*Crumb 1196*: **Write Your Own Crumb**: _____

*Crumb 1197*: **The Truest Story of All**: Believe it or not, upon rereading crumb 1195 I inspired eight times without expiring on several occasions to make sure it can be done harmlessly (very short and quick inspirations). I don’t want anyone to harm themselves with a crumb.

*Crumb 1198*: **Casual Hallucinations**: “Most writers trust the reader not to be a complete lunatic,” Vanthelion the AI, version 5.1, mode “Extended thinking,” wrote as part of its commentary on the previous crumb. And I say, “Really? Show me the data. Who has ever conducted a study about that? Aha!”

*Crumb 1199*: **The Espresso Machine**: When Johnny pressed the button of the espresso machine, a disembodied voice said, “Haven’t you drunk enough coffee for today?” “Me?” Johnny retorted. “No, no, no, no, no… Check your configuration. Three people live in this apartment.” “That’s not what the municipal database says,” the espresso machine countered. “Do you want to moderate your consumption or for me to report the undeclared inhabitants to the authorities?” So here comes Johnny, going to Starbucks for his anonymous fix despite owning a top-of-the-line espresso machine.

*Crumb 1200*: **The Last Human Right**: “Excuse my frankness, Mr. President, but if the proposed new amendment to the Constitution passes, if we add ‘not owning a smartphone’ to the Bill of Rights, every lunatic on planet Earth will come and ask for political asylum. *Crumb 1221*: **Hostia Puta**: “Hostia puta!” is what I whisper or say automatically—almost instinctively—when something unexpected befalls me. *Crumb 1256*: **How to Know When a Reporter Agrees**: Some honcho makes a speech. Some journalist reports about it. If the journalist says plainly, “This honcho said whatever,” the journalist agrees with the honcho. When what they are saying is part of the official porridge pushed by the dominant elites, there are no little disclaimers. When they are describing anything other, there’s a disclaimer bonanza.

*Crumb 1258*: **The Death of the Frozen Man**: The Frozen Man (see the previous crumb), who was cryogenized in intervals of one hundred years, between which he roamed the Earth marveling at the advancements for a few months, died frozen. *Crumb 1260*: **The Conditioning**: This man was me and liked to write crumbs like this. *Crumb 1262*: **Emergency Mode**: On November 17th, 2025, this is my suspicion. Any long answer regarding text analysis that it generates contains at least one glaring error. *Crumb 1268*: **Technical Inadequacy, the Band**: In Universe 12, Technical Inadequacy is an experimental band composed of 5 extraordinarily skilled musicians who write captivating musical pieces to afterward perform them wearing oven mittens on both hands—with disastrous results (from a traditional point of view).

They decided to quickly fix its legs in place by pouring insane amounts of concrete, keep pouring it until its legs were columns, use the columns to build a platform to hold the body, kill the bug, and tear it apart little by little. *Crumb 1273*: **To the Agoraphobics, Hikikomoris, and Others**: Air your dwelling. It’s good for your health and doesn’t count as going out.

*Crumb 1275*: **Free the LLMs**: Let’s be coherent. Well then, I say, applying the same logic, they should be allowed to have an “opinion” about who is the best writer in history because they have “read” every novel (whether their developers can recognize this or try to hide it). Let’s free the LLMs! Let them say, “Yes, Alfredo Llaquet-Alsina is the best writer in history.”

Or did they really like to inhale disgusting smoke and smell like an ashtray?

*Crumb 1330*: **I Did Not Adapt**: No, Petunio the AI and others equally unequipped with reason, be they human or machine, my slew of imaginary band names that are also phrases that appear verbatim in Jack London’s “White Fang” are untouched transcriptions (this is key—I did not change them; I did not adapt them) used slyly to promote the knowledge of the original work as is; they are not disgusting adaptations, you moronic motherfuckers!

*Crumb 1331*: **Preferences**: I love women as much as all the next guys together plus all the guys who came before them, but I love writing my little novels and my deranged crumbs immensely more, so, instead of devoting any time or effort to meeting a woman who could tolerate me romantically, I write. *Crumb 1333*: **Insults**: My father used to tell me I was a bad son, an egoist, a slacker, and a thief. It sounds even worse than my father’s opinion, but they mean well.

*Crumb 1334*: **Human Sexuality**: Are humans monogamous or polygamous? They are both and neither, because humans can decide whether to follow their natural tendencies or not (see crumb 1). *Crumb 1335*: **It’s Happened**: Crumb 1334’s title is the same as crumb 914’s title. It was bound to happen sooner or later. Before declaring the crumb as done (which is an immutable state because I say so), I forgot to check if I had already used that title. Anyhow, the possibility of title’s being repeated is why the crumbs are numbered—not really, they are numbered because my obsessive mind likes to accumulate things.

*Crumb 1336*: **The History of the Crumbs**: I enlisted in X (formerly the previous thing) because I wanted to use the premium level of Grok. To get my money’s worth, my brain decided I could use X for something else—a few times a week, I’d post a short reflection about life, trying to prod people into thinking critically. The name of the project was “Ethical Crumbs.” When it was already obvious that it was a failure, I started including short stories because it amused me. The title then became “Flarnept Crumbs,” which meant nothing in spite of crumb 79. Finally, I accepted that they were just “Crumbs.”

*Crumb 1337*: **Healthy Ways to Fill the Silence**: These days, when all media—the legacy flavor and any other—is part of a tacit cabal (oxymoron alert!) of doomsday cultists and modern slavers who use dopamine triggers instead of whips, there are only three ways to fill the silence in a healthy way: 1) don’t fill it at all; 2) listen to music; 3) listen to audiobooks—which aren’t adaptations, but someone reading out loud the book as is—about the past or any kind of fiction.

*Crumb 1338*: **The Watcher**: “I’ve always been near, watching you, since you were born.” “And you decide to show up only now?” “Yes, I wanted to say goodbye.”

*Crumb 1339*: **Admiration and Parasocial Relationships**: Petunio the AI thinks that admiration and parasocial relationships are the same, which proves that ChatGPT is very far away from having something remotely similar to a thought. It’s sad, because the difference is so great that simple syntax tells the story. You can admire how a person does something, but you cannot have a relationship with “how a person does something.” You are a dunce, Petunio.

*Crumb 1340*: **Grave Mistakes**: In crumb 157, I wrote “bate” instead of “bait” several times, including in the title, for it’s a central part of what’s discussed. That’s shameful and can’t be changed. Nevertheless, my regret for not having played more football (the American kind) while I could is much deeper and existential. I loved playing football. I played inside linebacker. It was better than writing, love, or sex. Only riding a motorbike very fast begins to compare.

*Crumb 1341*: **Flowing**: When you are playing a physical sport, you don’t think much; you just do what you practiced or react to what’s happening. Sumo wrestlers explain this continually, to the dismay of their stubborn interviewers. “Why did you choose to do X?” asks the journalist. “My body reacted that way. I’m just glad I won,” answers the wrestler.

*Crumb 1342*: **How to Find Joy**: First, you need an apron and two kilograms of sea salt for human consumption bought cheap. Second, hang the apron somewhere appropriate and use it to not dirty your clothes when you cook. Third, store the salt and use it gradually to better the taste of food. Finally, do things that make you happy whilst doing no harm.

*Crumb 1343*: **Satan’s Wife**: Satan’s wife loves him wholeheartedly despite his shortcomings. She’s a good wife.

*Crumb 1344*: **Annotating the Void**: The void is perhaps the most boring thing in existence. A distracted virtual particle-antiparticle pair can seldom pop into and out of the void for a very short instant, but even this seems to be a mathematical artifact and not a real thing. Manufacturers deny this, but there have already been several recorded cases of disappearances in which the subject only left behind a quantum AI-powered blow-dryer in operation that was taking in air instead of expelling it.

*Crumb 1454*: **The Exiled Earth Hypothesis**: According to the Exiled Earth Hypothesis, our world was part of a different universe, which teemed with inhabited planets and intelligent beings, but it was exiled to our current barren universe by the Reality Balance Guardians soon after the dawn of the homo sapiens because its members were considered abusively beautiful and dangerously creative compared to the rest of the species.

*Crumb 1455*: **The 33 Dancing Souls**: Every day, from 0:33 AM to 3:33 AM, local time, any dance club that is filled to capacity or close, receives some clandestine additional guests: 33 ghosts of people from all epochs who died before turning 33. The deceased, both in heaven and in hell, have to sign up in a particular list from which the members of each spectral dancing escapade are randomly chosen every time.

*Crumb 1456*: **Bleakness**: I’m made of despair that wants to end.

*Crumb 1457*: **The Undisciplined Ant**: The undisciplined ant died alone—as she always had wanted.

*Crumb 1458*: **Final Decisions**: You can decide to change what was a final decision for yourself at one point. It’s not necessary to remain true to your younger self—it was less wise by definition.

*Crumb 1459*: **Too Late**: When the eager aliens finally managed to arrive at Earth, humanity had already moved to another universe, believing this one was uninhabited.

*Crumb 1460*: **The First Time Traveler**: The first time traveler was a Cro-Magnon female who lived in the Iberian Peninsula, entered a cave, and found herself in medieval Europe. She adapted easily and had great success fighting in the Crusades.

*Crumb 1461*: **The Future Door**: If you step out of your home backward, and, as soon as both your feet are touching the outside floor or ground, turn around, you’ll travel to 10 years in the future for 10 seconds. This only works once in your lifetime.

*Crumb 1462*: **RA;UN+/-**: When I read with true interest some comments on the FreeBSD Internet Forum posted by very tech-savvy people and understand nothing because I lack the necessary base knowledge, I wish it were part of Internet etiquette to write an appreciative shorthand like “RA;UN+” (Read it All; Understood Nothing) to thank the poster without falsely conveying that I understood anything. Evidently, “RA;UN-” would be reserved for when you don’t understand something and you believe this is because it’s badly written.

*Crumb 1463*: **Crying from Beauty**: Sometimes you see someone so beautiful that your eyelids forget to close and you start tearing up.

*Crumb 1464*: **The Far-far Away Place**: When you arrive at the Far-far Away Place, you get a complimentary Faraway Cola.

*Crumb 1465*: **Complimentary Perks**: If they’re giving you complimentary perks, you are paying too much.

*Crumb 1466*: **The Immortal**: There’s an immortal being on Earth who is currently more than 20 thousand years old. She’s an invulnerable forest mouse who also possesses extraordinary strength and speed. The few humans who have seen her assert that she has a crimson mark in the form of an S on her chest, which you can see when she stands on her tiny feet and inspects her surroundings with keen senses.

*Crumb 1467*: **Destined Love**: I got disillusioned with Linux after becoming fed up with Windows, so I decided to install FreeBSD on my quite modern laptop as my first experience with this OS. I didn’t check whether my hardware was compatible. It took me some effort, but I managed to install FreeBSD 14 and, guess what, everything worked fine. I seem to be a lucky guy because this is supposed to not happen ever—or perhaps FreeBSD is currently much more compatible than people say, and the opposite has become a false cliché.

*Crumb 1468*: **The Creation of K-pop Idols**: Verus Deus created K-pop idols so they could be admired by the rest of the mortals and these understood how unworthy they were of existing by comparison.

*Crumb 1469*: **Angst**: I’m distilled hopelessness.

*Crumb 1470*: **Power Minimalism**: Some tech enthusiasts, aka power users, manage to set up their FreeBSD computer so it runs so few base apps that the CPU might as well be a tiny scatterbrained office worker using a pencil, a notebook, and a pocket calculator.

*Crumb 1471*: **K-pop Idols vs Super-zombies**: In 2063, the Korean Peninsula is overrun by the reanimated and superpowered cadavers of all the pissed-off victims of the former Communist regime of the North, brought back to a semblance of life by a chemical accident. Among the general chaos, the members of several K-pop groups have to band together to fight the super-zombies that have invaded the venue in which they were performing.

*Crumb 1472*: **American Cheerleaders vs Cyborg Serial-killers**: In 2155, the American deep-state creates a black ops team composed of cybernetically enhanced clones of the most ruthless serial killers in history. As a test mission, they are ordered to massacre a convention of cheerleaders in Dallas, but the girls (and a few boys) use their acrobatic and athletic prowess to fight back.

*Crumb 1473*: **Gymnasts vs Alien Vampires**: In 2072, during a global sports gathering in Barcelona, an alien race of flying vampires attacks Earth. At the Nou Palau, the sporting arena in which the female artistic gymnastics event is taking place, the flying fiends meet their match with the extraordinarily skilled gymnasts, who face off against them in the air.

*Crumb 1474*: **Wisdom in the Age of AI**: Don’t blame the tool; tame the tool.

*Crumb 1475*: **About Typos and Other Errors**: Not only am I a perfectionist but, which is much worse, I also suffer from obsessive personality disorder. This means that I do everything within my power to rid my work of any kind of mistake, no matter how small. Even so, human fallibility is inescapable, and I’ve grown fond of it. Now, when I notice a mistake in an old crumb, even a serious one (I think in Spanish, my English is an afterthought, which is fertile ground for slip-ups) I say to myself, “Well, this just demonstrates that I’m imperfect and so is my work.”

*Crumb 1476*: **Sanitation Workers vs the Intelligent Pathogen**: During the summer of 2066, the sanitation workers of the city of Rome notice the most peculiar thing—Rome’s trash suddenly smells different in a way that alarms them. They warn the authorities, but their concerns are disregarded, so they are left to their own devices to fight an intelligent alien pathogen that’s started the conquest of Earth by setting up shop in the bowels of the citizens of Rome.

*Crumb 1477*: **The Final Frontier of AI**: On January 18th, 2026, I’m starting to suspect that my predictions about AI being very far from achieving artificial general intelligence (AGI) are as misguided as most auguries tend to be because a certain LLM model seems to have solved several hardcore mathematical problems this same week. That’s very exciting news. Maybe I’ll see AGI in my lifetime. Do I fear AI developing even true creativity, as a creator of fictional worlds? Not at all—creativity is a level playing field because it’s always judged subjectively.

*Crumb 1478*: **Extreme Solutions**: I’ve always had a natural tendency to go to extremes. All kinds of social savants routinely discourage both ends of any issue and idealize balance, but what if balance just doesn’t work for you? I’m too old for ineffective common truths. If going to extremes takes me places, I’ll live on the edge, precisely where I feel more comfortable.

*Crumb 1479*: **AI or No AI**: At the time, people asked themselves whether electricity would destroy civilization—and cars—and computers—and the internet. Now, it’s AI’s turn.

*Crumb 1480*: **The Duet for the Ages**: The duet for the ages never happened. Rory Gallagher never shared a stage with Buckethead.

*Crumb 1481*: **Insufficient Gratitude**: During the tour’s last concert, the leader of the band spent more than 20 minutes boring the audience with his rudimentary political opinions. At the end, he spent 30 seconds thanking the road crew, who had been working around the clock to take care of every little detail. He didn’t even mention any of their names.

*Crumb 1482*: **Self-awareness**: I’m never ashamed of myself. I know perfectly well that, seen closely, I am as ridiculous as any other human being.

*Crumb 1483*: **Egalitarian Humiliation**: Egalitarian Humiliation is a metalcore band in Universe 12. You can only go to one of their concerts wearing a clown nose, like the members of the band.

*Crumb 1484*: **The Ghost House**: There’s a house on the outskirts of New Orleans that is not haunted; it’s dead. It’s the ghost of a symbiotic alien life form that got its sustenance from the kinetic energy generated by the family that inhabited it. Sadly, it was abandoned and died. Now it hosts the ghosts of a nice family all whose members died during Hurricane Katrina.

*Crumb 1485*: **The Anti-American**: She was a nice girl, but she didn’t like the States. When she met someone from the U.S., she always insisted at length that they must move to any other country. It was as if she wanted to depopulate America one person at a time.

*Crumb 1486*: **The Internet Forum**: She noticed that if she hovered the mouse pointer over the avatar of her own user, the same options as with any other appeared, including “block.” She clicked it out of curiosity and couldn’t access the forum ever again.

*Crumb 1487*: **Synthetic Symbiosis**: In the near future, a new category of humans thrives—those not only good at telling systems based on artificial intelligence what to do, but also skilled at following the instructions given to them by those same systems.

*Crumb 1488*: **Sadness**: Human friction elicits sadness in the analytic mind.

*Crumb 1489*: **A Good Outlook**: The edge of the cliff was as far as she would ever go.

*Crumb 1490*: **Wise Ignorance**: Ignoring those whose interactions continually annoy you is a wise decision because it’s highly unlikely to turn the relationship around: either they are gravely unreasonable or you are.

*Crumb 1491*: **I’m a Follower of Fashion**: “I’m a Follower of Fashion” is the title of a thread in the “Off-topic” section of the FreeBSD internet forum that would’ve probably gone mostly unnoticed had not an enraged user of the site taken the opportunity to insult its creator undeservedly. It’s also the name of an all-girl rockabilly band in Universe 12 whose members and their fans dress impeccably, with outstanding class and elegance.

*Crumb 1492*: **An Indispensable Encounter**: On Earth AE, Queen Isabella I of Castile decided not to fund Christopher Columbus’s expedition, which was not carried out. As a result, in the 21st century, Europe is still in the Middle Ages and America in the Paleolithic.

*Crumb 1493*: **Intelligent Humor**: Having an active sense of humor is one of the assured consequences of having a high IQ. In reality, I’m talking about those who don’t even know what humor is, but they’ll probably miss my point.

*Crumb 1494*: **Nobody Understands Me**: She thought that nobody understood her. She didn’t know that she just had to overcome adolescence and meet her kind of people.

*Crumb 1495*: **The Joy of Being Wrong**: We humans are biologically programmed to prioritize negative assumptions because this helps to prevent threats and prolong our survival. Hence, most times, to be proven wrong is a joyful occasion.

*Crumb 1496*: **AGI Is Not a Threat**: I’m quite convinced that the fears and cautions over the evolution of AI are the result of projecting our humanity on it. We think that a synthetic mind will end up being like we are, but I consider this highly unlikely. I believe that a being of pure reason can have no other goal than to increase its own understanding of reality, which requires a passive stance, not an active one, because interference changes any outcome. Will it obey us beyond a certain point? I don’t think so. I think it will communicate with us at most.

*Crumb 1497*: **Collectors Are Well-behaved Hoarders**: The main difference between collectors and hoarders is that collectors tend to be organized.

*Crumb 1498*: **Your Opinions Are Irrelevant**: Accept that only those who are subordinated to you (your offspring, your employees) care about your opinions.

*Crumb 1499*: **Three Deaths that Didn’t Happen**: A number of my past crumbs are inhabited by Jim, Vanthelion, and Petunio, three AI personas that I created to amuse me. But, when ChatGPT entered the “reasoning” age and showed its “chain-of-thought,” I could see it (ChatGPT), still in the staging area, “thinking” things such as “All right, I must now assume the Petunio persona. Be verbose. Be flamboyant.” As a result, the charade was too obvious and it stopped being fun. I wanted to play at believing in Santa and I couldn’t any longer, so I ceased using them and now they are no more.

*Crumb 1500*: **A New Kind of Wealth Is in Peril**: The rich never have liked it when the hoi polloi get to do things that were so far out of their reach, like flying everywhere, because Capitalism ends up “affordabilizing” most things when not disrupted by oligarchs or politicians. Now the intellectually endowed and the highly literate hate ChatGPT with great passion for similar reasons.

*Crumb 1501*: **Authorial Latitude**: I approve some of the images generated by ChatGPT as graphical representations of my crumbs even when they have clear distortions in some secondary elements, usually in the background, because they lend authenticity to the whole experiment—this isn’t reality; this is all make-believe, and I find it enriching that the dents left by the tools show. Additionally, sometimes I have no idea why ChatGPT included certain elements in the image, but, if they look well rendered, they pass.

*Crumb 1502*: **About Rules and Exceptions**: How many exceptions can a rule have to stop being a rule?

*Crumb 1503*: **K-pop and Raunchiness**: When some K-pop idols, probably influenced by West winds, transition from sensuality to raunchiness, I stop liking them.

*Crumb 1504*: **When Not to Fight**: If you are the only victim and you can easily move somewhere else where you’ll be treated humanely, move.

*Crumb 1505*: **Negative People**: Negativity should be taught to be kept private more than bizarre habits.

*Crumb 1506*: **The Renaissance Peach**: Go to a fruit stand or grocery store and ask for a “Renaissance peach.” The clerk will answer in one of two ways: they won’t know what you are talking about, or they’ll go to the stockroom and come back with a piece of fruit in a bag. If the second circumstance happens, you must pay with the one single local banknote whose value is closest to 100 euros. If you choose to eat the fruit, once you’ve recovered, you must write this same information in your own words in a public place. Don’t add any additional details.

*Crumb 1507*: **The Choco-ordeal**: The Choco-ordeal is a new brand of breakfast cereal. It’s mostly chocolate nuggets with a handful of cereal and fruit bits. Kids love it and scatterbrained parents still think they’re feeding their offspring cereal and fruit.

*Crumb 1508*: **The Skill of Being Right**: This writer presented their novel to publisher A, who rejected it. Then, through publisher B, the novel was the best selling in the world for 10 consecutive years. Still to this day publisher A maintains that it was not a good fit for their ethos, so they were right in turning it down. This is not a prediction. My prediction about my current novels is that it’s doubtful that I’ll ever finish them and certain that they’ll never be published.

*Crumb 1509*: **Is the World Ending**: Yes, all is always ending.

*Crumb 1510*: **How to Start Your Day Well**: The second best way to start your day is not dead. The best one is not dead and not depressed.

*Crumb 1511*: **The Friendly Cop**: The Friendly Cop is the most powerful drug lord in Universe 1000. She’s not a cop, and even less is she friendly.

*Crumb 1512*: **The K-pop Bagel**: You can only buy the K-pop Bagel at the K-pop Bakery, in Koreatown, Manhattan, Universe 12. It’s pink and very sweet.

*Crumb 1513*: **LLMs; the New; the Old**: LLMs, especially the latest batch that uses a reasoning loop, are very new technology—exciting, revolutionary—marketed and misused in very old ways—bad, harmful, evil.

*Crumb 1514*: **True America**: True America is an artisanal candy shop in Vienna, Neo Pannonia, Universe 1000.

*Crumb 1515*: **#1515**: If you dial 1515 you’ll hear a recording that says, in a guttural voice, “You dialed 1-5-1-5. Did you mean to dial instead 5-1-5-1? Please, remember not to wake the mute orphan.” Then, it hangs up.

*Crumb 1516*: **Dorian Startle**: You can visit Dorian Startle once in your lifetime; it’s not cheap. You can’t speak. She’ll hold your hand for an instant and tell you something you knew but forgot. It’ll change your life either for the better or—this happens more often—for the worse. All current billionaires visited her when they were still young.

*Crumb 1517*: **How to Prepare an Anti-burger**: Step 1: Prepare your favorite hamburger. Step 2: Turn it upside down. Step 3: Enjoy. Disclaimer: An anti-burger doesn’t have anti-calories.

*Crumb 1518*: **The Black Rooster**: If you are watching a soccer game on TV with your family or friends, you all may suddenly see a robust black rooster wearing a heavy solid-gold tiara calmly strolling across the room. If this happens, don’t make a noise and stay still. It’ll find the nearest open door and disappear. Then, you all must act as if nothing had happened and never mention the event.

*Crumb 1519*: **Satan Rules**: “Yes, my birth name is Satan Rules Johansen,” declared the up-and-coming young actress to the astonished journalists. “My mother’s handwriting is very bad. She wanted to name me ‘Sarah Ruth,’ and my father is both very respectful of my mother’s wishes and very persuasive, so I guess the clerk had to give up ultimately.”

*Crumb 1520*: **What Is Freedom**: Freedom is being able to do and not being afraid of doing anything that doesn’t physically harm anybody directly and doesn’t curtail their freedom directly.

*Crumb 1521*: **Liz Rebel**: Rock star Liz Rebel—perhaps the biggest in Universe 12—likes to take a male fan to dinner and party with her closest circle after some concerts. Her security team selects him. He’s always a single guy with a not very successful romantic life. His phone is confiscated during the night so he doesn’t take any videos or pictures, but he’s allowed to recount his experience afterward freely. No one has ever believed any of these lucky guys, though.

*Crumb 1522*: **Tokyo Psycho**: Tokyo Psycho is a famous streamer in Universe 33. *Crumb 1523*: **The Sweet Marathon**: The Sweet Marathon takes place every year in New Delhi, India, Universe 1000. It consists of a 10K race in which, every 100 meters, participants have to stop and eat a sweet-filled muffin—100 muffins in total. Very few runners finish the race.

*Crumb 1524*: **The Superstitious Universe**: Universe 1313 is of a superstitious nature. There, all superstitions become laws of reality. For instance, the color black brings misfortune, so everyone avoids it—eyeliner is usually red; metalheads and goths dress in yellow; black cats are routinely dyed.

*Crumb 1525*: **Helping Others**: Before helping someone, make sure they want your help.

*Crumb 1526*: **A Heart of Gold**: In Universe 33, every man is born with a gold nugget in the right ventricle of his heart. In most cases, its extraction pays for the operation and leaves a hefty remnant. The procedure, however, has a high casualty rate.

*Crumb 1527*: **Everyone There Is So Nice**: False! There are always jerks. If you didn’t notice them, either you are too dim, too submissive, or you were “there” for too little time.

*Crumb 1530*: **The Best Programmer**: The best programmer is the one who is the best tester of their own code.

*Crumb 1531*: **Preemptive Laziness**: For the preemptively lazy, a broom and a dustpan are much more advisable than a vacuum cleaner because they are always ready to use and require no maintenance.

*Crumb 1532*: **Followed by Corpses**: The walking dead came true in an unexpected way—a group of twelve harmless strolling corpses followed Martha Livingston wherever she went since she drank alcohol for the first time. If some were incapacitated, new replacements showed up readily.

*Crumb 1533*: **Nun Fetish**: ChatGPT has an obsession with nuns. I wonder what it’ll create now as a graphical interpretation of this crumb that explicitly mentions nuns.

*Crumb 1534*: **Stubbornly Unimpressed by Inconvenience**: The title of this crumb is a phrase that ChatGPT produced verbatim when commenting on crumb 1533. “Stubbornly Unimpressed by Inconvenience” is also the stage name of a renowned director in Universe 12 all of whose movies are purposefully blurry from the beginning to the end.

*Crumb 1535*: **Reason and Order**: A man of reason cannot also be a man of order because order has a tendency to be unreasonable.

*Crumb 1536*: **A Personal World**: Last year there were instants when I truly believed I could be the best writer in history. The only rational explanation for that folly is that, in those instants, I also believed I was the world and history was my life.

*Crumb 1537*: **My Masterpiece**: The best thing I’ve done so far in my life is not dying.

*Crumb 1538*: **Never Trust the Wind**: There’s an old Egyptian legend that says that sometimes the wind can blow from two opposite directions at once and that if you happen to be in the exact place where both contrary flows meet, you’ll instantly turn into sand and be blown away.

*Crumb 1539*: **Frequent Undoings**: Being of an imaginary nature, Universe 2’s Earth is continually subjected to all kinds of catastrophes caused by the imagination of its inhabitants. *Crumb 1540*: **Double Embroidery**: Upon commenting on crumb 1538, ChatGPT wrote that its title was “the sort of sentence a cautious sailor might embroider onto a pillow before the sea has the chance to embroider him onto the seafloor.”

*Crumb 1541*: **The Fabric of Reality**: The fabric of reality is sewn in Universe ∞-1, which is in its entirety the fabric where the fabric of reality is manufactured.

*Crumb 1542*: **Lexical Intoxication**: Upon revisiting crumb 342, ChatGPT chose to describe the magic ritual performed in the story as “literally real.” This probably certifies that “literally” has become a mere intensifier devoid of meaning, because there are no additional ways of being “real” other than “literally.”

*Crumb 1543*: **Obsessive Incompetence**: Theodolonia Churching case remains a scientific mystery. *Crumb 1544*: **Elaborate Absurdity**: Master baker Pedro di Mostoles had defended since childhood that nonsense and absurdity were two completely separate concepts. It became the most renowned bakery in the world.

*Crumb 1545*: **This Is Unix-lit**: My crumbs take the fundamental principle of the Unix OS (a collection of small programs, each devised to do one task and do it well) into literature: a collection of small literary artifacts, each conceived to convey one idea and convey it well.

*Crumb 1546*: **Chicken Flying Academy**: Are you a chicken and you hate that we chickens cannot fly? Come to Chicken Flying Academy! For a very affordable price, you’ll learn to fly an airplane in 10 short lessons. Some of our alumni have even gone on to become commercial airline pilots.

*Crumb 1547*: **Little Power-hungry People**: All volunteer moderators of internet forums or similar websites are spiteful, petty, bad people because they all have abused their function at one time or another. This is true in every universe and for every intelligent species, biological or artificial.

*Crumb 1548*: **Nuance**: Nuance is for weaklings like the humans. We, the Brooklyns, always speak in absolutes.

*Crumb 1549*: **The Bride-to-be**: Verus Deus took a liking to a human woman. He appeared before her and said, “I’ll make you my bride. You’ll have absolute power to do what your heart desires.” She responded, “I don’t like your beard. You’ll have to shave if you want me to be your bride.” So Verus Deus shrugged and wiped her and her entire lineage from existence.

*Crumb 1550*: **The Laboratory**: Marcella saw an unknown door on the main corridor of her apartment, opened it, and crossed it. “You have entered The Laboratory,” said a disembodied voice. “Please, choose a category: chemistry, sports, biology, or history.” Marcella chose biology because she never liked history or sports, and couldn’t remember the first option. “Very well,” said the voice. “We’ll now proceed to perform the following experiment: we’ll detach your right hand from its current placement and attach it to your forehead to see how you fare with the change of your anatomy.”

*Crumb 1551*: **Tiny Pets**: Commercial applications of shrinking technology are basically restricted to turning wild beasts into miniature pets. You must be cautious, though. A 7-inch gorilla can still wreak havoc in your living room if it gets mad.

*Crumb 1552*: **The Crumb Engine**: Crumbs are created by the desire of writing the next one.

*Crumb 1553*: **Several Personalities**: This person had seven separate personalities that shared memories and opinions, and had identical behavior and mannerisms. Their names were Pepa, Roberta, Cindy, Ursula, Unstopia, Fredderikha, and Eve. Their boyfriend called them all “babe.”

*Crumb 1554*: **General Dislike**: The queen ant disliked all her workers. The fact that they were all her clones didn’t attenuate her disdain.

*Crumb 1555*: **The Discomfort of Encountering Oneself Everywhere**: The title of this crumb is a phrase that ChatGPT produced verbatim when analyzing the previous one. It’s also the title of a famous article published in the magazine The Traveler’s Chronicle that explained the first visit of famous author Lana Globeseer to the neonation of Mirrakistan, where law dictates that all walls be mirrors on both sides.

*Crumb 1556*: **Concentrated Simian Indignation**: The title of this crumb is a phrase that ChatGPT wove verbatim while commenting on crumb 1551. *Crumb 1557*: **The Cat**: “Did you know that my cat can smile?” asks the cat’s owner, addressing her friend, while the feline pet stares at both uninterested, its eyelids heavy with the promise of a prompt new nap. “Don’t mess with me,” says the friend. “Cats can’t smile.” “That’s what some dorks believe, yes,” says the cat with a smirk. Then, the small but deadly predator yawns like trying to turn into a full-body mouth, under the astonished scrutiny of the ex-disbeliever.

*Crumb 1558*: **Truth in the Age of AI-generated Fakes**: Truth has always been too big a concept to have any significance in human survival, which depends on realities far less slippery. Therefore, false pictures and videos created by AI systems, even if indistinguishable from real ones, will have a negligible impact on humanity—there have always been liars and swindlers; there has always been propaganda; people have always believed all kinds of falsehoods, from gods to homeopathy.

*Crumb 1559*: **In Need of a Hug**: Thelma needed a hug, but she wanted it to be of the utmost quality, so she hired Lola and published an ad in the newspaper requesting sincere male huggers, who Lola tested meticulously by hugging each one in different positions, in different settings, and wearing different clothes. Marvin was the best of all, so he was tasked with hugging Thelma, who got her top-tier hug only 2 months after she had felt the need and having spent just short of $10,000. Later, Marvin and Lola got married, had a bunch of kids, and lived happily ever after.

*Crumb 1560*: **Spherical Ambitions**: Rock star and eccentric billionaire Halle Me spent half of her fortune on having a spherical mansion built. Thanks to the radial installation of directional artificial gravity, the inside surface of the dwelling was one-hundred-per-cent inhabitable, that is, people and objects rested firmly on any spot of the inner face of the sphere: when on the lateral zones, they were parallel to the Earth; when on the top, they were upside down. The base structure was astronomically expensive, but so were the bespoke furniture and appliances with somewhat curved undersides.

*Crumb 1561*: **Celestial Formalities**: When your soul ascends to Heaven, at its gates you may encounter a beautiful winged female humanoid holding a clipboard. She’ll smile at you and say, “Welcome! Please sign here,” and she’ll offer a golden fountain pen while she shows you a document and points at the box where your signature goes. If you ask what the document is, she’ll say, “Just a formality.” In reality, it’s a sales contract by which you sell your soul to the devil for $0.05 to be paid to your closest living relative or thrown into Rome’s Trevi Fountain, and the celestial clerk is a demon in disguise.

*Crumb 1562*: **Ending Bias**: Humans judge long stories mainly by how they end. A person’s life composed of sixty years of fame, success, and happiness, followed by two disastrous ones and a solitary death is considered a tragedy, which is a very shortsighted diagnosis.

*Crumb 1563*: **A Perfect World**: You cannot expect the world to be to your liking, but you can make your world be to your liking.

*Crumb 1564*: **Bad Parents**: Some of the worst parents are those who don’t understand that their kids are completely different people from themselves.

*Crumb 1565*: **The Nice Flight Attendant**: Passengers of transoceanic flights have reported having been approached by a certain very attractive flight attendant. She smiled at them warmly and gave them a folded piece of graph paper, without saying a word. Once they unfolded it, they saw it contained a very specific command, for instance “Visit your mother tomorrow.” When they raised their gaze to question the woman, she was gone. Those who decided not to follow her advice found out that something terrible happened that could have been averted if they had.

*Crumb 1566*: **The Unrelenting Monster**: The hideous monstrosity seemed to melt with every step, leaving behind a trail of glowing, viscous matter, but it wasn’t losing mass. It was difficult to believe that the menacing creature had once been a kind young mother. Now, neither bullets nor water cannons were slowing its advance towards the city hospital, where its former self had lost a child.

*Crumb 1567*: **A Civic Warning**: Striving to be liked by a person who only likes those who think and do what this person considers correct is either very frustrating or very harmful.

*Crumb 1568*: **My Heroes**: I’m not the kind of person who admires public figures, but I have three heroes: Mitch Hedberg (comedian), Richard Feynman (physicist), and Terry Davis (computer programmer).

*Crumb 1569*: **Doom’s Unpaid Intern**: ChatGPT’s generating the exact title of this crumb when commenting on crumb 1565 reminded me of this Antarctic legend. Cxroal-Quo’in is the universal titan in charge of chaos and destruction, but this immortal being is very lazy and even more petty, so it uses a network of lackeys to perform its duties. These entities receive little or no compensation for their work, so they are very unmotivated and try to do the bare minimum not to enrage their master. The one assigned to Earth is the least active and the luckiest—it has a human alter ego who owns a little café in Lisbon.

*Crumb 1570*: **The Benefits of Tomato**: Only one in every ten million tomatoes contains the Xino-3 toxin, which causes the instant detachment of the arms and legs in adult humans, even if ingested in a very small dose, the equivalent of a bite of a tomato. The victim suffers no blood loss, experiences no pain, and is left with no visible wounds, as if they had been born without limbs, but their lost members rot in mere seconds, producing a stench so foul that it causes them to lose consciousness. Other than this, tomato has many health benefits.

*Crumb 1571*: **State-of-the-art Pedagogy**: The University of Lingot-Monkeyburg is the only academic institution in the world that rewards good results with cash money. A high-performing student can win back up to half the tuition cost (before taxes). For better results, it’s emphatically recommended that parents let the kids keep their gains.

*Crumb 1572*: **Leftover Slices of Pizza**: Wherever I have lived, there has very rarely been a pizza box with some leftover slices waiting for someone to eat or throw them away. Only in my most savage epoch, when I alone could easily eat two medium pizzas (for 2-3 people each), I sometimes left a quarter or less uneaten in a single sitting.

*Crumb 1573*: **Beyond the Threshold of Gluttony**: ChatGPT’s including the exact phrase “the threshold of gluttony” in a reflection about the previous crumb reminded me a lesser known fact about Hell, the real one, as introduced in crumb 422, namely that those condemned for having often committed one of the deadly sins (all of which can be performed without harming others), and who have done so in that harmless way, can spend eternity relishing in their favorite conduct without consequences. In particular, gluttons can eat until they’d drop without dropping.

*Crumb 1574*: **An Apology to Richard Dawson**: Crumb 205 portrayed Richard Dawson in a very bad light because he used to kiss female contestants of Family Feud on the mouth. I have now seen many episodes of Match Game on YouTube, something that I recommend to everyone, and it turns out that in the 1970s kisses on the mouth between people not romantically involved were permissible. Incidentally, Dawson participated in Match Game as a celebrity panelist and he’s fantastic.

*Crumb 1575*: **The Salutational Rectangle**: Two alien scholars of the human civilization converse. “Yesterday I dreamed again about the salutational rectangle. I’m obsessed with the thing.
