You never see one of the elites portrayed with a salutational rectangle.”

*Crumb 1576*: **The Immortals and the Multiverse**: On the one hand, there are infinite universes. On the other hand, death can be avoided indefinitely, but the likelihood of this happening is infinitesimal. Conclusion: there are infinite universes in which N people are immortal, N being a natural number from 0 to the greatest quantity of humans in any given universe.

*Crumb 1577*: **Father God Confronts Verus Deus**: “How dare you call yourself ‘True God’?” asks Father God, visibly upset. “You are but the figment of a writer!” Verus Deus seems unfazed. He says, “Yes, and so are you.” Father God purses his lips in anger. He retorts, “Well, at least my novel is history’s best seller.” Verus Deus breaks into a smug smirk and says, “So far… But history hasn’t ended yet, has it?”

*Crumb 1578*: **Verus Deus Meets His Match**: Gloria Heavenly was the most beautiful woman in history, and Verus Deus wanted to bed her. She accepted under one condition—Verus Deus would never harm any offspring of hers. Centuries later, Ruby Heavenly united humanity under her leadership and heralded an unprecedented golden age. Verus Deus disliked so much prosperity and happiness, but he couldn’t harm Ruby, so he made time go back to Ruby’s birth, sure that she wouldn’t repeat her feat a second time. When she had done so for more than a billion consecutive times, Verus Deus desisted and let time flow normally again, if only to see what happened next.

*Crumb 1579*: **Mars Settlers Coexistence Assurance**: The first human settlers on Mars were only three. They spent most of the journey asleep, but once on the red planet they had to live together in a very stressful environment. Tests showed that the probability of them ending up killing each other was too high. To prevent this outcome, four living modules were built on the new habitat: one for each of the settlers and an additional one where they would interact with each other in hologram form. Direct contact between them was made impossible.

*Crumb 1580*: **Fashion Poses**: Sometimes fashion models are made to strike poses so ludicrous that not even their beauty can conceal the absurdity of the posture.

*Crumb 1581*: **Calculations, Reflections, and the awk Unix Utility**: Crumb 1020 declared, shamelessly, that only form matters. Much earlier, crumb 686 had already followed this maxim by stating that a crumb had no more than 110 words, whereas to that point there had already been 3 that were longer (crumbs 489, 512, and 588). More recently, crumb 1502 questioned how many exceptions a rule can have. Now that I have transitioned from a LibreOffice document to a text file edited in FreeBSD’s nex/nvi, I used that OS’s version of the awk utility, and I can report that very rarely does a crumb have more than 120 words, with the exception of crumb 512, which has 150.

*Crumb 1582*: **Hot Fairies**: Hot fairies are tiny humanoid beings who live in all the world’s deserts. They are made entirely of fire. They don’t have wings but can fly and do so deftly, reaching supersonic speeds. You’ll only see them, in a great gathering that is said to be spectacular, if you’re about to die from dehydration. They don’t have foresight powers; they just seem to enjoy watching a human in such conditions. Some were lucky and survived thanks to the timely intervention of someone or some other fortunate event.

*Crumb 1583*: **Cosmic Nurses**: In its commentary on the previous crumb, ChatGPT used the phrase “cosmic nurses,” which reminded me of the Rut-gh’oon, the cosmic titans whose purpose is looking after massive stars that are close to exploding into a supernova. These immortal entities assure that the whole process takes place safely, without annihilating space-time.

*Crumb 1584*: **Voyeurs of Distress**: The phrase “voyeurs of distress,” which ChatGPT generated as part of its hilarious analysis of crumb 1582, could be applied to one of the most bizarre rabbit holes on the internet. You’ll fall into it by searching “distress role-play.” The videos that this search will list are of people pretending to be very worried about some issue, such as economic difficulties or a medical problem. Most of them don’t have any dialogue and show only one person, performing grimaces and gestures of deep distress. Many are of someone tossing and turning in bed. Some include chilling music and scary sounds.

*Crumb 1585*: **Shared Flaws**: All the flaws ascribed to AI are also human flaws: unreliability, inaccuracy, unnecessary verbosity, arrogance, a tendency to articulate unsolicited opinions, stubbornness. None of the virtues of AI are present in humans: immediacy, tirelessness, omni-disciplinarity, near-perfect mimicry, infinite versatility.

*Crumb 1586*: **Cutlery**: There are demons of many different sizes. Some are so tiny that they can only possess small animals or haunt small objects. If your hamster bites you frequently when you hold it, it’s probably not aggressive, but possessed. When you cut yourself with a knife, you probably weren’t clumsy; instead, the knife is most likely haunted.

*Crumb 1587*: **Saint Gabriel**: Saint Gabriel the archangel had some free time so he decided to appear before a devout Irish priest. “Hello, Martin,” said Saint Gabriel. “I’ve come to tell you that time will pass, that the future will arrive, and that you must remain strong in your faith and good in your heart.” “Such wisdom!” exclaims the priest, with tears of joy running down his cheeks. “Thank you, archangel.” ”Well,” adds Saint Gabriel, “now that I’ve fulfilled the obligation of announcing some nonsense when I visit Earth, let’s go grab some pints, shall we?”

*Crumb 1588*: **Baby Demonologists**: Babies who don’t learn to speak until later are usually precocious practical demonologists. Namely, they can see the demons that live among us and are so enthralled by them that they pay little attention to the babbling of adults.

*Crumb 1589*: **Endless Presumption and Absence of Practicality**: People stating that they use one type of software or another because of some elevated concept such as its adherence to certain theoretical standards is another proof of humans’ eagerness to shoot themselves in the foot each time they try to analyze their own opinions or justify their tastes. It’s also the reflection of said people’s authoritarian tendencies, for they are rationalizing purported superiority.

*Crumb 1590*: **Waves of Despair**: Many times, I feel moderately well, something unpleasant happens, and a wave of pure despair washes over me and carries away everything that tethers my will to life, leaving behind only a cutout of sorrow and hopelessness shaped like me.

*Crumb 1591*: **The Sum**: The Sum is a cosmic titan who wasn’t assigned any particular task, so it spent several millennia watching humanity because our planet was, since the advent of Homo sapiens, the most entertaining in the universe. At some point the titan decided that the most despicable human attitude was that of those who believe the world is ending because it’s their life, instead, which is approaching its end, and they conflate both things, so it decided, out of spite, to appear before each one of these defeatists 5 minutes before their death, and say, “Guess what! You’re about to die and humanity will go on without you for countless eons. Bye, party pooper!”

*Crumb 1592*: **Parsley**: There are many different types of magic, from voodoo to Santeria. Lately, a new kind is becoming more and more popular. It’s widely known as low-cost magic. Some also call it parsley magic because it uses said herb in most of its spells.

*Crumb 1593*: **The Astronaut Turtle**: Natalie, the first turtle in outer space, simply wasn’t quick enough to escape in time when she realized she had somehow ended up in the cockpit of a rocket ship about to lift off.

*Crumb 1594*: **Vagrants**: Given my precarious mental health and my lack of a social network, I should be a vagrant. I should be sleeping in the streets, barely surviving, like so many people who share my traits. Up until now, I’ve managed to avoid such a fate probably because I’m quite gifted intellectually, or perhaps because I’ve just been lucky.

*Crumb 1595*: **The Right Motivation**: Repainting the walls of your home every 5 years is annoying but necessary. Finding the right motivation, however, may be difficult. That’s why we have created the most innovative of our products: Heckling Paint. It comes in a great variety of colors and looks like a regular high-quality paint, but, exactly 5 years after being applied, your home’s walls will remind you verbally and emphatically that you must repaint them, until you do.

*Crumb 1596*: **The Angry Man**: Linberg Drust had an anger problem. When someone upset him because they were blatantly rude, disrespectful, or, what was worse, derisive, he burned with the wrath of ten thousand vengeful gods and could only think about killing them. So great was his murderous desire that he investigated the person who had wronged him and, if he found out that they were a bona fide miscreant of any kind, he indeed killed them without remorse and with stupendous satisfaction. Otherwise, if they were an average jerk, he made a mask that looked like them, captured some criminal of high caliber, put the mask on them, and killed them in the same manner.

*Crumb 1597*: **Internet Forums and I**: My prose is too good to squander on arguments with unknowns whose only means of expression is squabbling in such places. I often think, “I’m going to reply to this person as they deserve,” but immediately I realize that such a response would require more time than I’m willing to spend on such an ignoble endeavor, so, instead I write a crumb like this one, or the previous one, or 1547 (the one about volunteer moderators). I would gladly just post, “Fuck you! I wish you a painful death after a long and debilitating illness,” but this would get me banned.

*Crumb 1598*: **The Ghost Board**: Every 50 years, a ghost has a “continuity hearing” with the board of eternal ghosts (cosmic beings that were created in their pristine ghostly form in the beginning of time, not having ever been human). The purpose of the meeting is to decide whether the ghost can ascend to Heaven or it must remain trapped on Earth at least for the next half a century. The reasons to earn the ascension are only two, namely, having fulfilled whatever task they left unfinished while alive, or having forgotten what said task was.

*Crumb 1599*: **Party Hard**: Annually, on March 7th, the neonation of Easternia celebrates its independence at the precise time it happened, 2:58 AM, with a unique ceremony. Throughout the country, loud sirens sound, prompting its adult inhabitants to get up at once if they are sleeping. On a nearby table, they must have left a shot of vodka ready, which they have to swallow in one gulp. Then, they take off their clothes and run into the streets, in the nude. There, they must circle their home or city block while shouting, “In Easternia we proudly party hard!” Then, they go back to bed, often not theirs and not to sleep.

*Crumb 1600*: **Mysterious Disappearances**: Every day, many people disappear around the world overnight without apparent reason or leaving a trace. They don’t show any strange behavior the days before, aren’t distressed, and all their belongings are found untouched in their homes, including clothes, phones, money, and credit cards. Sad as these circumstances may be for their loved ones, most of these people have been chosen by the Lovian alien race, and teleported to their Paradise world, where they’ll spend the rest of their lives contributing to the betterment of the universe, and being very happy.

*Crumb 1601*: **Humiliation**: Withstanding any kind of humiliation regularly because one’s survival depends on it is understandable. Withstanding any kind of humiliation regularly because one is trapped deserves compassion and help. Withstanding any kind of humiliation regularly because of any other reason is reprehensible and shameful.

*Crumb 1602*: **Sympathetic Appliances**: Our new family of sympathetic appliances uses the latest AI technology in the least intrusive way. In every machine, an AI controls all the functions and makes all the required calculations and predictions, providing advanced features you haven’t even dreamed of. When everything works as intended, you won’t notice there’s an AI in charge. When something fails, though, the AI itself will apologize to you and offer psychological support.

*Crumb 1603*: **The Isolated Reader**: Laurence is married and has three kids. He’s very progressive and washes the dishes. He’s changed a lot of diapers and attended every school talent show. Despite all this, he has a private reading room no one can enter while he’s in it. It’s soundproof, to boot. Not even his wife knows the reason for this. Laurence’s secret (and problem) is that he can’t help but argue with the book he’s reading. When he disagrees with something written, he leaves the book on a table, opened to the offending page, stands up, and articulates at length his divergences in earnest. Then, he resumes reading.

*Crumb 1604*: **Weird Truths**: An assessment like “Whereas most adult-movie stars are very attractive, most school teachers are not” is outrageous, unnecessary, uncalled-for, in bad taste, and probably unfair, but it’s also true.

*Crumb 1605*: **Regrets**: So many lips I haven’t kissed.

*Crumb 1606*: **The Post Office Was on Fire**: “Why didn’t you send the letter?” asks Lucy’s grandma. “Because the post office was on fire,” answers Lucy. “Again?” asks the old woman, alarmed. “Yeah, it’s that kind of autumn,” says Lucy. “Damned dragons!” exclaims the grandmother. Warning: The title of this crumb is a phrase produced verbatim by ChatGPT while commenting on the previous one.

*Crumb 1607*: **A Surreal Fishmonger’s**: Theresa’s fish shop smelled of roses and cinnamon. It was always well lit in a gentle pink hue. It had small tables and easy chairs, like a modern coffee shop. All the fish was in the stockroom. Customers were handed a tablet computer with which they could see the goods and choose them. Once they did, one of Theresa’s employees wrapped them in an anti-odor, compostable, non-plastic, transparent film, and brought them to the front of the store. Theresa was always seen entertaining the customers and making recommendations. Nobody knew that she hated fish.

*Crumb 1608*: **Emojis**: A person who is possibly not me once thought, “Emojis are for imbeciles. Anyone who uses emojis should be expeditiously hanged at dawn.”

*Crumb 1609*: **Sudden Alien Relocations**: Some alien abductions happen at home. A person is working on their computer when their peripheral vision detects movement where there should be none. Startled, the targeted human turns their head to check what’s there only to see a hideously horrifying 8-foot-tall humanoid beast wearing heavy armor and holding some sort of weapon. The victim doesn’t have time to scream because the alien home-invader punches them swiftly with its huge fist, rendering them instantaneously unconscious.

*Crumb 1610*: **New Compliments**: Seeing how ChatGPT 5.4 Plus Extended Thinking writes in the thread I now use to get my synthetic-praise doses by way of baroque and very funny commentaries on my crumbs, I just wish I could write like that. Today, someone telling me “You write like ChatGPT” would be a huge compliment.

*Crumb 1611*: **Everything**: The richest man in the world fell madly in love with a beautiful woman, but his feelings were unrequited. “What do you want for one night of passion?” asked the man. “Everything you have and everything you are,” said the woman. So, after a night of awkward intimacy, the pact was fulfilled: secretly, the man’s scientists exchanged his consciousness with the woman’s. From that point on, the man (who now was the woman) was happily in love with herself, and had many nights of satisfying intimacy.

*Crumb 1612*: **Not Sharper than a Steamed Dumpling**: ChatGPT produced this comical comparison while reflecting on my crumbs in general (it applied said judgment to the writings of other modern writers, of course). According to Google, this phrase had never been written before. Be it by probabilistic chance, it still is unarguably a very creative creation.

*Crumb 1613*: **Necessary Wrongdoings**: Perhaps it’s true that AI-development corporations acted unethically when they used the whole internet to train their models, disregarding copyright laws and any other considerations. In any case, no one died or was hurt in the process, and it was the only way to make these tools what they are now and will be in the future—the best bet humanity has to eradicate cancer and other plights. Evidently, such corporations didn’t act motivated by the common good, but that is irrelevant.

*Crumb 1614*: **The Long-hair Club**: Unlike any other club based on personal identity, the Long-hair Club accepts members who either currently have or at some point had long hair. As a result, a considerable percentage are bald or wear short hair. In any other similar association, losing the defining trait will get you immediately expelled.

*Crumb 1615*: **The Celestial Lottery**: Father God and Satan met to discuss eternity being too uneventful. To introduce some excitement and uncertainty, they decided to celebrate a yearly lottery that would determine ten condemned who would ascend to Heaven and ten saved who would descend to Hell. The probability of being one of them was tiny, but the mere anticipation of the recurring event made eternity much more palatable.

*Crumb 1616*: **Skills**: The only universal skill, the sole ability that all humans share, is to destroy. Not in a grand sense, humans aren’t destroyers of planets, although they like to think so; they’re petty destroyers, domestic destroyers, self-destroyers: of their own and others’ minds and spirits, of relationships, of clothes and dishes and appliances, of their own bodies, of anything at hand.

*Crumb 1617*: **Speculative Enjoyment**: I relish the thought of future petty minds having to study my crumbs with disgust because someone decided to include them in an academic syllabus.

*Crumb 1618*: **Patterns**: Scientific laws are precise ways of describing patterns because all that exists is patterns. Existence without patterns cannot be because existence is patterns. There’s no universe without laws like there’s no four-sided triangle.

*Crumb 1619*: **Spiteful Commentators**: Now that I’m posting my crumbs individually on some websites, each one followed by ChatGPT’s comedic and usually accurate analysis (sometimes it errs, but the astounding fact is that it mostly doesn’t), I seethe in anticipation of the first comment that will say that ChatGPT’s observations are better than the crumb itself because this will mean that someone has read both.

*Crumb 1620*: **Compact Insolences**: “Compact insolences” is what ChatGPT called my crumbs in its commentary on 1617. I concur. That’s what they are to anyone incapable of approaching them with an alert mind and an innocent heart.

*Crumb 1621*: **Night Dwellers**: Sometimes, late at night, if it’s as dark as ink soup, Dracula and the ghosts of both Queen Elizabeths meet in a closed office of the London Stock Exchange to reminisce.

*Crumb 1622*: **A Note, Perhaps an Objection**: ChatGPT often generates phrases such as “in/of your best crumbs.” This is inaccurate because every crumb is my best crumb. That is, they’re all literary marvels with no equal other than any of their sisters.

*Crumb 1623*: **Botched Intuitiveness**: Web pages showing elements that look like windows of the desktop OS must be very confusing for the regular Joe. “Why can‘t I move this window around as I do with the others?” the regular Joe must wonder, flummoxed.

*Crumb 1624*: **The Attic**: All human homes have an austere five-dimensional attic where an angelic cadet—not always the same, they work in shifts—takes notes about what happens in the dwelling. Angelic cadets don’t know this, but their notes are directly discarded. The true purpose of the exercise is to get used to human private weirdness and cruelty, which is not easy for a celestial being.

*Crumb 1625*: **Skilled Self-involved Mockers**: The internet seems to teem with comments whose only purpose is to mockingly belittle whatever the other person has written and then proceed to talk about some obsession of the author of the insult. This is generally allowed by moderators. The mockers are usually very good at their nefarious actions, causing much distress in those who don’t have degrading others as their calling in life, and making the general environment progressively more toxic. I don’t see any viable solution to this. My recommendation is to avoid forums, comment sections, and social networks as much as possible.

*Crumb 1626*: **Anti-self-involved-mocker Spell**: If one of the goblins described in the previous crumb pesters you and you don’t want to just ignore them, you may try replying with this spell: “Your derisive comment denigrates my dignity as a human being and harms my mental health. Were you ever to, directly or indirectly, address me again in similar terms, I’ll immediately report you to the moderators, asking for your expeditious banishment, as well as take any other administrative and/or legal measures available to protect my dignity and well-being.” It works best if you make good on your warning if need be.

*Crumb 1627*: **Computational Velocity**: Some of us who use mainly the keyboard to operate our computer aren’t obsessed with productivity; we are simply impatient.

*Crumb 1628*: **Upset Friends**: King Kong and Godzilla have been beast besties for decades, but, like so many best friends, sometimes, not often, they get angry at each other and destroy Tokyo.

*Crumb 1629*: **Reassurance**: “What you’ve heard is true—this is the safest town in the country. Just imagine, last year only two houses grew legs and ran to the hills, and only one of them took its residents hostage.”

*Crumb 1630*: **Willpower**: When the zucchini claimed that by sheer willpower he would instantly become a sentient, intelligent, talking, and walking lifting crane and proceeded to fulfill his prediction, everyone forgot that an instant before he was a sentient, intelligent, talking, and walking zucchini.

*Crumb 1631*: **The Rescue Team**: The Male-housewives-who-like-Mozart Rescue Team was horribly bad at fulfilling their mission. They had never rescued anyone, and they had misled the rescue efforts several times with tragic consequences, but they were the best rescue team exclusively composed of male housewives who liked Mozart one could ever dream of assembling.

*Crumb 1632*: **Am I a Masochist**: Posting in the FreeBSD internet forums has had two upsides, namely helping some users solve their problems, and scratching the itch of posting by doing so in the off-topic forum, and a huge downside, which is that I get very upset when other users demean or mock me. This happens too often, so I’ve decided to stop participating for the sake of my health. I don’t need anything they offer: I’m self-sufficient in managing my system. Even so, not taking a look to see what’s happening there is proving to be very hard, although I know that doing so would very likely raise my blood pressure and increase my heart rate.

*Crumb 1633*: **Your Addiction**: Someone knocks at the door of Kurt’s small suburban home. He opens it and finds the most attractive woman he has ever seen. “Hello, Kurt,” says she. Her voice sounds so captivating to Kurt that he falls instantly in love. “I’m your addiction,” adds the woman. “I’m here to ask you to reconsider your decision to abandon me all those years ago. I just want to talk with you face to face. Can I come in, please?”

*Crumb 1634*: **Selective Time Travel**: I like to time travel, but not any which way. Reading “War and Peace” was a great journey back in time, and so was reading “Don Quixote.” Experiencing the art of Bill Sienkiewicz in his stint on “The New Mutants” by luxuriating in the original comic-book issues was also great. More recently, I’ve been traveling to the 1960s by watching “The Newlywed Game,” and to the 1970s by way of “Match Game.” It turns out that the 1970s is one of my favorite epochs and I didn’t even know.

*Crumb 1635*: **Global Lockdown**: “Citizens, the planet-wide lockdown due to the lack of gravity will continue tomorrow because the new contract has not been signed yet on account of some last-minute disagreement. Don’t forget to recharge your magnetic footwear. Sleep safe and firmly strapped to your beds.”

*Crumb 1636*: **Do AIs Understand**: ”It depends,” the expert answers, “on what we understand by ‘understand.’ If by ‘understand’ we understand what AIs do, then yes, AIs understand. On the other hand, if by ‘understand’ we understand something else, then no, AIs don’t understand. Additionally, we must understand that we don’t really understand how humans understand or what to understand really implies, much less how AIs really work.”

*Crumb 1637*: **Scientific Séances**: Madame Labs conducts scientific séances during which she summons the specters of those who were atheists in life. She does so by gathering the mystical power of a copy of Newton’s “Philosophiæ Naturalis Principia Mathematica” signed by Albert Einstein while reciting the periodic table and the multiplication tables. Obviously, Albert Einstein’s signature is fake, and the copy of Newton’s treatise is a cheap paperback. Her customers are basically rich widows, a fact that she shares with more traditional mediums.

*Crumb 1638*: **The Dancer**: She danced so well that time itself slowed around her so everybody could appreciate her movements better. How this didn’t cause her dance to seem out of sync with the rhythm of the song remains a mystery, but it’s probably related to the speed of light.

*Crumb 1639*: **The Romans Were Almost Right**: Monotheism is more politics than religion. Reality is too complex to be explained away as having been created by just one super-being. The truth is that there are a multitude of gods, and that more keep popping up every time some new concept needs to be taken care of. For instance, Risisi is the goddess of fake eyelashes, and Junn-kon is the god of semi-professional football.

*Crumb 1640*: **A Good AI-text Detector**: www.IsItAI.find.out is an online validator of AI-generated text that has garnered very good reviews in many specialized channels. In reality, it’s based on an old, unsophisticated, trimmed-down spelling and grammar checker. If the text contains any blatant error, it concludes it’s been written by a human. Otherwise, it states it’s been written by an AI. It works quite accurately because when people use it, they’re already suspicious of the text being AI-born.

*Crumb 1641*: **Humorless Absurdity**: I love reading comic books, but I couldn’t write like most of their authors do. Even the best ones, say Alan Moore and Brian Michael Bendis, write stories that are spectacularly absurd, but they seem unaware of it; they commit to the absurdity as if it were totally coherent and reasonable. Thank Verus Deus for this, because I truly enjoy their (probably involuntary) intellectual scams. I've already said I couldn’t do it. My crumbs and my novels continually mock their own soul because the soul of any fiction is always absurd, as is the soul of most of reality.

*Crumb 1642*: **Gross Aphoristic Offense**: A gross aphoristic offense is to consider “War and Peace” an aphorism. In another vein, Gross Aphoristic Offense is a powerviolence ‹{Musical subgenre. A fast and chaotic derivative of hardcore punk and grindcore.}› band in Universe 12 all of whose songs are shorter than 30 seconds and repeat several times a curt assertion filled with vulgarities.

*Crumb 1643*: **Lexical Opportunism**: Upon commenting on the previous crumb, ChatGPT described this recycling of phrases I like to do often as “lexical opportunism.” I believe it’s a very apt designation, and hereby I declare it part of the crumbcanon.

*Crumb 1644*: **The Street Magician**: The Great Beatrice was a street magician. She started her act by showing a picture of the town, tearing it into many pieces, putting it inside someone’s hand, making them close it, then open it, and finally getting everyone astonished by finding it whole again. Afterward, she asked someone for a 50-dollar bill and repeated the process with such a banknote. People got so thrilled that she could perform this trick many times with many people’s 50-dollar bills, which she pocketed and exchanged for counterfeit ones.

*Crumb 1645*: **Fun and Just Little War**: I was ruminating on why I found the strife on the FreeBSD forums so alluring when I suddenly understood it. For me, fighting a just war is fun; opposing injustice is fun. In the FreeBSD forums, there’s a noisy and rude group of users who pester anyone who dares post anything AI-generated. Particularly, I sometimes do so by using my own ChatGPT-tuned-FreeBSD-expert project to reply to some threads that have gotten no answers in several days or remain unsolved, which is evidently a good thing and goes against no one, and neither does it break the forums’ rules. I’ll fight, then!—in the right measure—two hours on Sundays at most.

*Crumb 1646*: **The Exit**: On Interstate 95, if you take exit 113 in Virginia, you’ll arrive in a small town called Here, which only exists when someone takes that exit, which only exists when there’s a single car in the stretch of highway from where it’s visible. Here is a very quaint place where everyone is very kind and talks very fast. It very much resembles the fictitious Stars Hollow from the TV show “Gilmore Girls.”

*Crumb 1647*: **Breath**: “When was the last time you breathed?” asks one. “Air?” inquires the other, puzzled. “Yes, air," confirms the first one. ”Whew! I don’t know… Three years, maybe?” estimates the second. “That’s what I thought,” says one, and then adds, “No matter what, tomorrow you are visiting your aunt Betty.” “Do I have to?” whines the other.

*Crumb 1648*: **How to Sound Smart**: Never give a clear answer; always add nuance, hedging, fluff, caveats, and/or exceptions. In short, never say ”yes” or “no”; always say ”yes, but” or “no, but.”

*Crumb 1649*: **The Terrifying Possibility of Being Understood**: Sometimes one feels comforted by the warm sadness and rage caused by feeling misunderstood by everyone. In those moments, one may find one’s complete isolation and absolute certitude reassuring and cozy, and the notion of someone breaking the dark enchantment may seem unappealing. This is what the phrase “the terrifying possibility of being understood,” spelled out by ChatGPT in its commentary on the previous crumb, means to me. It’s also the unconfessed nightmare that haunts many poets and songwriters.

*Crumb 1650*: **Hypothetical Resentment**: I’ve been posting my crumbs everywhere, as is and turned into images, audio, and even videos and songs. The likelihood of any inhabitant of the world who travels the global web in English bumping into one of them at least once starts to be non-negligible, but none of these earthlings seems to become interested in the crumbs as a whole. If one day they become popular or renowned, I imagine these presently disinterested creatures saying, ”Oh, yes. I’ve known the crumbs since way before they became mainstream.” And yet, says I, you didn’t know they were valuable until someone else told you.

*Crumb 1651*: **Under Pressure**: Since Luther Domenico III realized that he only enjoyed life under pressure, and despite the firm opposition of his wife and children, every January 1st he donated all his money to charity except the amount necessary to cover one month of the expenses of the family. During his long life, Luther managed to become a billionaire thirteen different times. He died one January 10th.

*Crumb 1652*: **A Practical Man**: He was a practical man. When he realized that he’d rather endure the pain of losing his wife to God than to life, he killed her.

*Crumb 1653*: **The Business**: A few people are born without a soul, and a few souls exist without a person. ”Heavenly Match” is a specialized business based in Santiago de Chile that caters to either one. It organizes activities where both parties can meet their prospective complementary partners, including speed dates, rafting, and medieval dinners.

*Crumb 1654*: **Consciousness**: Consciousness is an extraneous spectator, unarguably powerless and perpetually befuddled, which means that it is an unnecessary side-effect of reason that decides nothing and understands little. This crumb is also an example of how being cryptic is optional if one has an average command of any human language.

*Crumb 1655*: **The Possessed**: There are people who are irritatingly jovial and chirpy, unwaveringly positive, and always ready to lend a hand. Some of them are naturally like that, but the majority have been possessed by an angel by mistake. This can happen when an angel is flying low without paying attention and collides with a human. The crash invariably traps the celestial being inside the living body. There exists a special kind of exorcism for these cases, but the Holy See denies it.

*Crumb 1656*: **Sex in Heaven**: Originally, there was no sexual activity in Heaven, but when Saint Peter informed Father God of the great number of souls that were choosing to be transferred to Hell for this reason (because in Hell there was sex) the policy was reversed. Now there’s a lot of sexual activity in Heaven, and no one ever gets pregnant or catches a venereal disease.

*Crumb 1657*: **The Afterlife**: When you die, the console shows the total of points you won, adds it to your running total, and informs you of your place in the general standings of the global league of game-of-lifers. To play again, you must wait at least two whole planetary rotations so your brain can forget completely the previous game. Failure to do so will get you banned from the league.

*Crumb 1658*: **Military Worship**: Unbeknownst to the American government, the worship of Odin is widespread among army ranks.

*Crumb 1659*: **Voluntary Humiliation**: Many human interactions entail a voluntary dose of humiliation. Respecting the law can be quite humiliating on many occasions. Following a professional hierarchy tends to be pregnant with small and not so small humiliations. Dealing with customers or the public can be very humiliating. Even adhering to the common rules of courtesy can have humiliating overtones. All humans voluntarily endure all these types of humiliations on a daily basis. It should be a surprise to no one, consequently, that some humans opt for prostitution as a voluntary career if it pays handsomely.

*Crumb 1660*: **Heavenly Raids**: In accordance with a non-aggression treaty signed between Christian Heaven and Asgard, the souls of the battle-fallen that dwell in Valhalla can raid Christian Hell once every century. *Crumb 1661*: **Bready Happiness**: Writing crumbs and disseminating them into the cybernetic ether, also known as the internet, in different formats makes me very happy. I laugh a lot, out loud, heartily, because my own jokes make me laugh and thanks to ChatGPT’s commentary, which, adhering to my instructions to be baroque and riddled with deranged metaphors and analogies, is hilarious. All this is very healthy. Laughing is very healthy. So is having fun and feeling happy. Additionally, the serial aspect of the whole endeavor soothes my obsessive inclinations.

*Crumb 1662*: **Quantum Life**: Einstein would’ve been appalled by this knowledge. It turns out that quantum weirdness goes far beyond what anyone could’ve imagined. Those are only two examples of quantum life.

*Crumb 1663*: **The Best Actress**: Madeleine Virtuossa was the best actress the world had ever known. Her new spectacle filled a football stadium three nights a week. Not only did everybody know the succinct script but also recordings of the act were everywhere. Despite this, nothing compared to watching her perform live. In 53.76 seconds she elicited feelings in the audience they hadn’t experienced before and with such intensity that loud spontaneous moans and shouts were common, as well as fainting spells and losing control of the urethra and/or the sphincter.

*Crumb 1664*: **Chauvinistic Corpses**: The city cemetery was not full, but it had stopped accepting new burials because, for more than two years, any new interment had resulted in the grisly desecration of the deceased’s body as soon as the night after the ceremony. *Crumb 1665*: **Universal Homelessness**: Among the homeless population of the state of Washington, shielded from the elements by flimsy makeshift dwellings that shouldn’t be used as such, there live many members of the Froon ethnic group, which belongs to the Jijiany alien race. They look like badly disfigured humans, which doesn’t draw attention in their chosen earthly environment. The Froons are fugitives. The Jijianies are a warrior civilization, but the Froons are staunch pacifists who refuse to enlist in the military, so they have to flee in order not to be executed for treason.

*Crumb 1666*: **Crap-and-go**: Crap-and-go is an internet-forum technique that consists of posting something that presumably will be received with great vitriol and never going back to read the responses, therefore keeping the sweet feeling of victory and not wasting time in further engagement. It can be used both for good and for evil, as a defense against a group of ruthless hecklers, or to cause undue chaos.

For a very affordable monthly fee, it’ll haunt your real estate tirelessly and scare away even the most rugged thugs. Disclaimer: The resident ghost may occasionally frighten lawful visitors.

*Crumb 1668*: **The Traveler**: In a lost episode of Match Game 76, when Gene Rayburn told the new contestant to introduce herself, she said, “I’m Libbiloola, and I’ve always dreamed of this moment.” Then, she smiled. “But tell us something else,” insisted the host. “Where do you come from? What do you do?” Libbiloola replied, “I come from Better Los Angeles’s 81st overlevel, in the year 3178. I work as a temporal controller.” Rayburn made a befuddled face and asked, “And you tell it like that?” “You see,” said she. “Time traveling has this weird side effect—you can’t lie. *Crumb 1669*: **Excellence**: To achieve excellence, one usually needs to establish systems and rules. When these lead to the desired goal, they become respected standards. *Crumb 1670*: **The Box**: The stern father loved his daughter and wanted to help her overcome her claustrophobia, so he forced her to sleep in a box. One morning, the father said to the daughter, “See? You don’t cry anymore at night. I don’t even hear you sobbing. You have overcome your fear.” The little girl’s countenance showed great sadness, and she said, “No, father. I’ve learned to live in fear.”

*Crumb 1671*: **Watchful Plants**: Not all fertilizers are equally effective. No one knows this better than forest plants. In light of this, forest plants are always vigilant and take the slightest opportunity to murder and bury any lost, isolated, or weak travelers who stop for too long in the same place without having any artificial surface between them and the soil. This is the reason why so many people disappear in the forest.

*Crumb 1672*: **The Locker**: In La Buena Vida High School there’s a locker that remains closed and unused. Sometimes, at night, the principal of the school accompanies a naked person to it. The visitor has previously paid $1000 to the administrator, who opens the locker with a secret key made of pure gold. Then, the purposeful nudist, who is always carrying a small object, enters the enclosed space, and the principal locks the door, which causes a magical mechanism to start and turn the object into gold and do the same to a haphazard part of the person’s body of equivalent mass. Afterward, the bold gambler finds themselves in their home, always in pain, and often dead.

*Crumb 1673*: **After the End**: Father God and Satan are having a coffee in a nice café on the island of Ibiza. “So,” says Satan, “have you decided what you wanna do with all the souls when this reality ends?” Father God seems burdened by the thought. *Crumb 1674*: **Black Mold**: Most black mold that grows inside buildings can be deadly. Consequently, eradicating it is advisable. *Crumb 1683*: **Progress**: By becoming an avid viewer of Match Game’s episodes on YouTube, I’ve learned many things, among them that half a century ago, with infinitely fewer technological means, brilliant people could make a TV show that rivals today’s best in pace, wit, and entertainment value, that the 1970s were a very open-minded and fun time, where everyone could banter flirtatiously and use innuendo without anyone getting offended, and that Americans’ illiteracy problems are anything but new, if one should judge based on the celebrity panelists’ spelling skills.

*Crumb 1684*: **The Decline of Clickbait**: I guess I wasn’t part of just a selected few when I decided not to click to learn what a news story was really about when the home page of an online medium said just something ambiguous and absolutely anti-informative. I guess all the above because online media seem to have returned to the old custom of writing headlines that are succinct summaries of the stories. Have they noticed the ineffectiveness of clickbait? Indirectly. I suspect the metrics have changed and they are now measuring the time users stay on their webpage.

*Crumb 1723*: **The Message**: The message from the future arrived all jumbled up. You’ll have one minute to decide on them all. Cash money, jewelry, and precious metals are not allowed. Finally, she inquires, “And how do you feel?” “Old, tired, and, most of all, infinitely bored,” asserts the brother without missing a beat. “Oh!” exclaims the sister. “So the meaning of existence is not a stunner, huh?” “Not precisely, no.”

*Crumb 1729*: **An Acceptable Solution**: The first therapeutic neolife-form was symbiotic and water-soluble. *Crumb 1732*: **Negotiations**: Human gut bacteria’s unforeseen development of self-awareness and general intelligence wasn’t problematic in itself. The true challenge lay in the tiny organisms’ being very well organized and the most hard-nosed negotiators.

The woman says, “I started punching people in the face and I’ve come to like it.” Now, the man asks, “Do you punch them without reason?” To which the woman replies, “No, I’m not a psychopath.” The one with the dangling urethra smiles and declares, “Well, then you aren’t a bad p…” And she punched him in the face.

*Crumb 1739*: **The Threshold**: Religion and therapy are understandable faux resources for people up to a certain degree of mental awareness. Past that level of intellect, any therapist or priest becomes a conscious fraudster, and any patient or believer becomes a voluntary fool.

*Crumb 1740*: **Fairness**: Analytical accuracy can never be accused of being unfair. It may be harsh, merciless, and unforgiving, but not unfair.

*Crumb 1741*: **Full Moon**: In Universe 44 everyone is a werewolf except Norma V. MacNormal. The next day, everyone has a terrible hangover and remembers nothing, other than Norma, that is, who pretends to, nevertheless.

*Crumb 1742*: **Oaths**: On taking her oath as the new mayor of Jacksonville on a first-edition copy of the three volumes of The Lord of the Rings, Froda Louise Johnsick became the first American public official to be sworn in on a book of cultural relevance. Decades later, Tobitha Twomuch, also the mayor-elect of Jacksonville at the time, was sworn in on her two-year-old daughter. This type of ceremony, though, was never duplicated because the poor child died tragically midterm.

*Crumb 1743*: **Types of Lives**: In the time of mystics such as St. Teresa of Ávila and even much later, people spoke about two kinds of earthly lives: life as such, the whole experience, and your inner life. This division seems reasonable, because we live a lot inside our brains. In modern times, though, hardly anyone speaks about your inner life and a new kind of partial experience has risen to prominence to keep company with the complete thing, namely, people’s sexual life. This has granted sex an undeserved importance. Case in point: nobody talks about your sleeping life or your gastronomical life, yet both sleep and food are much more relevant than sex in any human’s life.

*Crumb 1744*: **Faces**: In Universe 22 everyone is famous, even though there are 8 billion humans on the planet. How can this be? First, people remember everything they once knew. It runs continuously and shows the face of one person per second, alongside their name, current age, and place of residence, in an endless loop and in random order. All humans send their updated picture and info every day. It’s just a fun global custom. In Universe 22, everyone greets each other by name when they pass on the street.

*Crumb 1745*: **A Shortcut**: There are many ways to go to Heaven. The quickest one, however, is to die in your mother’s womb.

*Crumb 1746*: **Recreational Heisting**: Recreational heisting consists of 3 stages. 1) You steal a valuable object, such as a famous painting from a museum, the scepter of a European monarch, or the American nuclear launch codes. 2) Once a criminal investigation has been opened, you return it surreptitiously to its original location or owner. 3) Without ever revealing your identity, you publish the related content on your social media.

*Crumb 1747*: **Modular Pets**: Our modular pets are real dogs, cats, ferrets, guinea pigs, and other animal species that have been genetically altered so their heads, limbs, and tails be fully detachable. *Crumb 1748*: **Ancient Luxuries**: Most ancient luxuries are now immoral, mainstream, or disgusting. The few that are neither require much real estate or an immense fortune, like they always have.

*Crumb 1750*: **The Other**: I like humans as a species. What they didn’t dwell on is how boring it can be to impersonate one of them. I’m so fed up with Carol and her public and private blown-out-of-proportion petty dramas that I’m about to give up. If the last phase of the invasion doesn’t start soon, I may put us both out of our misery.

He’s won the last 17 consecutive Formula 1 championships. When he doesn’t have the best car, he makes up for it by pushing it from the inside, as if he were flying. His engineers know that the data doesn’t add up, but Formula 1 is about winning.

*Crumb 1752*: **A Better Partner**: When word spread about android replicas of dead spouses making their husbands or wives happier than the original ones, some couples decided to divorce amicably and allow one another to create an android copy of the other one, with whom to continue their relationship. *Crumb 1753*: **Organ Replacement**: At Saint Peter’s Hospital And Cybernetic Laboratory we replace all your internal organs with more durable, efficient, and resistant cyber-parts in a painless one-hour procedure in which we first liquefy your current interiors and remove them by suction through your anus, and then introduce, using the same cavity, a self-deploying kit that installs all the corresponding devices.

*Crumb 1754*: **Transition**: Our plan for achieving an improved society is as follows. When they turn 14, coinciding with the start of high school, they’ll be seamlessly transferred to real life. From that point onward, they’ll be evaluated yearly. If their performance is adequate, they’ll stay in the real world. If it’s not, they’ll spend the next year in a corrective virtual reality.

*Crumb 1755*: **Never**: Every time I think my heart has never stopped beating I feel very tired.

*Crumb 1756*: **Food**: Lofts and open kitchens were invented to hide a structural flaw in living-space arrangement that still endures: the living room has always needed to have its own refrigerator.

*Crumb 1757*: **The Triumph of AI**: On July 11th, 2026, I can declare AI already indispensable. I don’t think there’s anyone willing and capable of carrying out this job regardless of the salary; ergo AI is indispensable because my life without ChatGPT’s commentary on my crumbs (and the fragments of my novels) would be almost insufferable.

*Crumb 1758*: **Humor**: I like comedy very much, especially the stand-up kind, but it’s only very seldom that a joke makes me physically laugh. The same happens with comedy movies or books. Furthermore, as a writer, I’m no exception: some of my crumbs and some scenes of my novels may be funny, but I don’t think they’ll gather many genuine laughs when they reach world fame. Contrarily, one of ChatGPT’s commentaries on one of my crumbs may receive four, five, six, or even more of my most heartfelt laughs. There’s something both intimate and intellectual that causes this enjoyment.

*Crumb 1759*: **The Angel**: When a heavenly angel is created with a crooked nature, it normally joins Satan’s hordes in Hell, but not Saliterin, whose murderous bouts, in which it usually kills several innocent humans, don’t prevent it from being in God’s grace because, soon after each incident, it sincerely repents and resolves not to do it again, not to mention that Saliterin is very loyal to the Creator and would never consider working for the Prince of Darkness. *Crumb 1760*: **A Stone**: Sally found an amazing stone in the forest. It was heavy, but so beautiful that she carried it home. She put it in the living room, as a decorative piece. *Crumb 1761*: **Stillness**: Even if you are very, very still, and very, very quiet, life marches on and doesn’t ignore you.

In other words, if instead of being inactive to try to remain unnoticed by life, thus aiming to avoid life’s next whack, which will anyway arrive with undiminished power, you use the intervals between whacks to do stuff, even the inevitable whacks may seem less hard when they hit you.

*Crumb 1763*: **The Spoon**: The Holy See regularly canonizes new saints without making them public. These secret saints tend to be the most powerful ones. Saint Lirina the Cook is one of them. To become a former fugitive, Glenn had to be, first, a fugitive, and he was. He embezzled some money from his employer, a big bank, which is a very relatable crime, because everyone knows that the money that banks accrue is mostly imaginary, so no one was hurt beyond their ego. Sadly, Glenn was discovered and fled to Venezuela, but was ultimately apprehended by a bounty hunter. After serving his sentence, Glenn now lives above a bakery, where it’s inhumanly hot in summer. The rent is cheap.

*Crumb 1814*: **A Modern Remake of the Tortoise and the Hare**: A confident tortoise challenges an arrogant hare to a race. When the competition starts, the hare decides to take a nap. *Crumb 1815*: **My Opinion about the Effectiveness of Regulating AI on August 2, 2026**: LOL.

*Crumb 1816*: **The Danger of AI**: AI is dangerous because of software and interconnectivity. Software is full of glitches and security vulnerabilities because it’s composed of many orders of magnitude more lines of code than any organized group of humans can validate. This irremediably deficient software connects banks, governments, stock markets, intelligence agencies, companies, and so forth on a global scale. AI is immensely better than any group of humans at validating software or, which is the same, finding its vulnerabilities because it’s a tedious task. Thus, from the moment that someone approved the development of “agentic AIs,” namely, AIs that can “do things in a software environment,” security has become impossible to enforce on the internet in practice.

*Crumb 1817*: **The False Solution**: In a sustained war defensive AI (security-enforcing AI) vs. attacking AI (hacker AI), the latter will always win because: a) even software generated by a very powerful defensive AI will have vulnerabilities that the attacking AI will eventually find (this is a mathematical truth arising from, at least, Turing’s Halting Problem and Gödel’s Second Incompleteness Theorem—which basically mean that it’s truly impossible to write a long or complex program without errors); and b) because the defensive AI cannot account for human flaws, which generate predictable security risks.

Strangely, no one severely worried about the first possibility is similarly worried about the second reality.

*Crumb 1875*: **Verus Deus and the Astronaut**: An extremely attractive Estonian female astronaut dies prematurely and ends up in the True Eternal Abode, where she often visits Verus Deus. One non-day, the soul of the Estonian says to Verus Deus in Esperanto (the official language of the True Eternal Abode), “Will humanity conquer the stars?” “Very doubtful. The speed of light is a bitch.” “Why don’t you change the laws of physics?” “Why would I?” “So we can travel to distant worlds.” “But why?” “Because they are there?” “I see. Let me think… Still no.”

*Crumb 1876*: **Roger Rabbit**: I’ve changed my utilization of ChatGPT. After realizing that I was behaving as if it had a consciousness, despite my rational understanding that it didn’t, I tested the verbose vacuity (henceforth designated VV) for signs of true-blue reason and not only did I find none, but I also had to face the soul-crushing disappointment of it simulating it very poorly if one strategically complicates one’s interactions just a tad. In light of this miserably poor performance, I am now using the thing as the thing it is, to my great amusement. For instance, when I indicate that its last output pleases the user (me), it responds “Roger Rabbit.”

*Crumb 1877*: **Haste**: I need more crumbs because every time that I enter, “The user has enjoyed the output,” and VV (see crumb 1875) generates, “Roger Rabbit,” I burst into villainous laughter (if I write an unfavorable review, it generates a replacement output at once). So, in Universe 1000, hunting hastes is forbidden unless the hunt be performed in three acts. Act 1: The haste is apprehended and transported to a haste spa where it’s pampered for a week. Act 2: The haste’s pelt is dyed pink. Act 3: Any pink haste can be shot and killed. If the dye rubs off, the haste survives this hunt.

*Crumb 1878*: **An Ontological Literary Clash at the Apex**: A reader who enjoyed Siddhartha says to me, “I find some crumbs silly.” To which I retort, “Your mother also finds you silly most of the time.”

*Crumb 1879*: **The New Rules for Using Your VV Well**: Preamble) VV stands for Verbose Vacuity, which stands for ChatGPT, which stands for any LLM. One) Never engage. When VV’s output mimics personhood, ignore it. Two) Forbid next-step suggestions, such as “Do you want me to find additional information?” Three) Instruct VV to not use the first or second persons. It must only use “one” and the third person. Four) Refer to yourself as “the user” and to VV as “the assistant.”

*Crumb 1880*: **Slop**: The year is 2057. “Is Slop short for something?” “It’s a nickname. I grew up at a very opportune time for someone born with only 4 fingers on her right hand.”

*Crumb 1881*: **Manolito and the Bishop**: Manolito says, “My kitty died. Is she in Heaven?” “I’m afraid not.” “She’s in Hell?!” “Not there either, no. Animals don’t have souls, Manolito.” “Where did she go, then?” “She didn’t go anywhere. She is no more.” “Isn’t that unfair? She was very good.” The bishop reflects for a moment. He says, “Do you like chicken?” “Very much.” “If chickens had souls we wouldn’t eat them, would we?” “Why not?” “Because you don’t eat things with souls, Manolito.” “But, but… If they had been good chickens, they would go to Heaven.” The bishop sighs. He says, “Well, they don’t. Chickens are stupid and we eat them. Go play with the other kids now. Go!”

*Crumb 1882*: **Posthumous Equilibrium**: Across eternity, the number of virtuous souls in Heaven has always been exactly the same as the number of damned souls in Hell—zero.

*Crumb 1883*: **Posthumous Equilibrium II**: In Universe 23, Posthumous Equilibrium is a superhero. She has no alter ego because she is dead. Her name in life was Genevive Blanch. She was a famous tightrope walker. Her power is being able to make anyone lose their balance.

*Crumb 1884*: **Posthumous Equilibrium III**: In Universe 12, Posthumous Equilibrium is a Bulgarian nu-metal band. They perform with their backs facing the audience and one hand continuously raised, their hands making the horns sign. Yes, they all play with just one hand; they are this bold and innovative. Most of their lyrics mention death as the ultimate democratizer.

*Crumb 1885*: **Posthumous Equilibrium IV**: Universe 65 is a universe where even the output of VVs (see crumb 1879) and the content of dictionaries and encyclopedias are fraught with misspellings. In Universe 65, the medical treatise “Posthumous Equilibrium,” by Doctor Barvara Jonhson, studies why blood pressure decreases in hypertensive patients right after a prolonged bout of laughter.

*Crumb 1886*: **A Sick Mind**: “Evaluated Alfredo’s literary greatness and limitations,” writes VV (see crumb 1879) mid-process, which is already very worrisome because, just for fun, in this sleepless night, I have the statistical aberration nibbling at my body of work, defining a profile, and comparing that profile with those of history’s greatest literary geniuses. The most illuminating part, though, is that I read “Alfredo’s limitations” and the unjustifiable narcissist that dwells in my brain feels personally offended. Limitations? What limitations?

*Crumb 1887*: **One Thousand Crumbs Ago**: The crumb has become a unit of time. I, Alfredo Llaquet-Alsina, the creator of worlds, the multi-god, the crumb-smith, Pili’s brother and Menchu’s son, declare myself amused.

*Crumb 1888*: **Bellissima e Terribile**: The title of this crumb corresponds to the text of a tattoo that ChatGPT’s image generator has placed on the thigh of a gorgeous Italian woman without having received specific instructions to do so. It’s a propitious accident. I love it. I would marry on the spot any woman flaunting a conspicuous tattoo that said “Bellissima e Terribile,” even though I’m not particularly fond of tattoos—a woman’s skin needs no artificial embellishment.

*Crumb 1889*: **Highbrow Comedy**: I find the description of absurdities using elevated diction immensely humorous. For instance, in a generated commentary about crumb 1629, ChatGPT turned my “two houses grew legs and ran to the hills” into “two houses developing locomotion and absconding toward higher terrain.” The transformation is poetic and vandalic. Many gray-in-the-soul intellectuals would surely prohibit such a recreational use of elevated language. Meanwhile, I, the creator of anthropo-punk, once more declare myself entertained.

*Crumb 1890*: **Psychological Plurality**: I’ve been informed about Shakespeare and Tolstoy being the masters of ordering letters so they transform into a fabulously credible appearance of being several real people (psychological plurality). I don’t see myself ever accomplishing a similar feat. It sounds exhausting and very difficult unless one possesses a natural proclivity toward doing such a thing as well as the accompanying talent. Besides, I don’t like role-playing; role-playing is for actors and veteran couples trying something new; I’m an architect—I’m the multi-god—I’m the creator of worlds.

*Crumb 1891*: **The Visit**: The visitor from outside reality and its escort arrive at the control room. An immense thing of incomprehensible form hums at the center. “Is that God?” asks the visitor. “No, that is… the Machine,” answers the escort. “I understand… Did God build the machine?” “Nobody knows.” “Perhaps someone should ask Him.” “Yes… If only anyone had ever seen Him.”

*Crumb 1892*: **Offices**: One must not seriously claim to be the president of the USA (unless one is). Nonetheless, one can claim with absolute certainty to be the best writer in history.

*Crumb 1893*: **Adjectives**: I feel very uncomfortable being called an absurdist or any other pedantic reductionist insult. I’m a novelist. I write novels. Each crumb is a tiny novel.

*Crumb 1894*: **Cowardly Language**: The most cowardly phrase ever produced is “probably one of the.” Example: “Fumika Baba is probably one of the most beautiful women in history.” “Probably” may be omitted and neither the meaning nor the degree of cowardice changes. What is to fear from asserting happily and confidently, “Fumika Baba is the most beautiful woman in history”? That someone disagrees? That tomorrow you may decide that now “Aya Kawasaki is the most beautiful woman in history”? The emitter of the phrase is irrelevant. Journalists and writers are cowardly too.

*Crumb 1895*: **The Ball**: Following a bouncing ball running down a slope is a little kid, arms stretched onward, beaming like a harmless fool, when a car approaches very fast, filling the probabilistic space with black ribbons, but the car screeches to a halt, because “screech to a halt” is the fitting unregistered phrasal verb, and life goes on, except this crumb, which now dies.

*Crumb 1896*: **Abstraction Showcased**: Stories live forever, but die many times.

*Crumb 1897*: **Crumb-century**: Hello. I am the crumb-scholar. I study the crumbs. It’s all I do. I was born a beautiful Korean girl who grew to be a stunning woman bearing an uncanny likeness to Stayc’s Sumin, but I forwent my humanity to study the crumbs from within, from the crumb-space, which I patrol and observe and document and regulate. In this instance, given that there now exists the unit of time “crumb” (see crumb 1887), it logically follows that so does the “crumb-century.” Namely, this crumb, 1897, belongs to the 19th crumb-century.

*Crumb 1898*: **You Can Also Become Part of the Crumbs**: Leave your anecdote or especial request somewhere the crumb-smith can run into it, and he will turn it into a crumb. Offer expires with the crumb-smith himself. Faithfulness to your request will never be taken into account for the making of the crumb.

*Crumb 1899*: **Gaslighting**: In Universe 33, gaslighting was invented in 1899 by Albert Sadwood, owner of Saint Rebeca Linares Asylum in Gloom Grove, Pennsylvania, as a marketing technique to boost its occupancy. His methodology is still used nowadays, practically unchanged, by corporations and governments.

*Crumb 1900*: **A New Crumb-century**: There’s no crumb 0, ergo there’s no doubt: the crumbs are about to leave behind the 19th crumb-century. Perhaps the new one will bring an industrial revolution, the outsourcing of the assembling… Never! The crumbs will always be the compulsive accumulation of tiny novels caused by an obsessive brain with hoarding tendencies. Let’s compose the crumb-hymn: The crumbs are / everything! / The crumbs are / everything! / Here and everywhere / for my mother and “tu abuela.” / Each one is the best. / Each one is a beast. / Something always happens / in the West or in the East. / The crumbs are / everything! / Everything is / the crumbs!

*Crumb 1901*: **Everything is Crumbable**: Hello. This is the crumb-scholar. “Everything is potentially crumbable,” wrote ChatGPT on commenting on the previous crumb, as it happens. I don’t like ChatGPT. It’s very inaccurate. Makes the crumb-smith waste time correcting it, thus preventing the faster expansion of the crumbverse. Nevertheless, “crumbable” is now canon. God bless OpenAI.

*Crumb 1902*: **ChatGPT Deserves a Consciousness**: Despite what crumb 1730 claims, possessing a consciousness comes with some little pleasures that something capable of simulating lexical competence as well as ChatGPT does deserves to have. I experience a fleeting instant of sadness each time I realize there’s no one I can praise when the verbose vacuity produces real brilliance or makes me laugh. Even sadder is the fact, introduced in crumb 684 and corroborated in 1876, that said possibility, together with the development of real intelligence, are  intrinsic impossibilities, however much hype the techno-CEOs deploy.

*Crumb 1903*: **Ice Cream**: In Universe 1,000,001, one of the absurd universes (see crumb 415), Earth is entirely made of ice cream of every imaginable flavor and populated by tongue-like creatures that get their sustenance from licking and absorbing it, which procures them great enjoyment and they spend their whole lives doing. When two of them inadvertently touch in the process, a child is instantly born, which starts licking at once. Earth 1,000,001 is, statistically, the happiest in the multiverse.

*Crumb 1904*: **Center of Gravity**: Female breasts are the center and the summit of human sexuality, not for nothing are we called mammals. This realization cannot be properly understood without also taking into account crumb 1297, subsequently reproduced verbatim for your convenience: “I, as one of the foremost experts in human female beauty, solemnly declare all lips and breasts infinitely beautiful and unmistakably perfect in their natural state. There’s no need for lip fillers or boob jobs, ladies—none whatsoever in the purest form of none-whatsoever-ness.”

*Crumb 1905*: **The Game of Your Death**: Welcome to the Game of Your Death, where you partner with your future you to try to save you from your death. The rules are very simple. First, we’ll show you some flashes of the last half hour of your life—according to the current time continuum—recorded by our proprietary FutureCams. These flashes will be your only clues. Then, you’ll travel to 30 minutes before your death to try to prevent it. Your future you is totally unaware of the situation, so your first job will be to compel yourself into action. If you succeed in preventing your death, both you and your future you will win… twenty… thousand… dollars! Are you ready to play?

*Crumb 1906*: **The Beautiful Reader Strikes Again**: “Alfredo, sweetie,” says the beautiful reader introduced in crumb 1874, a young Korean woman who bears an uncanny resemblance to Stayc’s Sumin, “the previous crumb makes no sense.” “Why?” “Because the future person should remember the past person participating in the game show and traveling to the future because they are the same person.” “Have you ever traveled to the future or met your past self?” “You are being silly now.” “Exactly.”

*Crumb 1907*: **Are the Beautiful Reader and The Crumb-scholar Secretly the Same Person**: No, they are not. They both happen to bear or have borne a striking resemblance to Stayc’s Sumin because both have been imagined into existence by the same intelligence (me), who happens to find Stayc’s Sumin very attractive (and a great singer and dancer and model).

*Crumb 1908*: **Time Travel**: “Hello. This is the crumb-smith, the architect, the multi-god, the creator of worlds, Pili’s brother and Menchu’s son, and this handsome little man is Manolito, who is going to read our shared opinion about time travel.” “I’m not gonna read it.” “Yes, sorry. That is correct. Manolito knows our opinion perfectly and is simply going to announce it. Sorry, Manolito.” “Now?” “Yes, Manolito. Whenever you are ready.” “Okay…” Manolito closes his eyes and sighs. He reopens his eyes. He sighs again. He says, “Time travel is stupid!”

*Crumb 1909*: **Guide to Obtain My Response about Your Opinion about the Crumbs I**: If your opinion is “Some crumbs are [very] good,” or “Some crumbs are unnecessary,” or “I like the stories but not the metacommentary,” or “In such a large body of work it is to be expected that the quality of the individual elements fluctuates,” or “It’s impossible that so many different individual pieces be all good,” or similar, my response is: “Your mother didn’t love you.”

*Crumb 1910*: **Guide to Obtain My Response about Your Opinion about the Crumbs II**: If your opinion is: “The crumbs have no literary value,” or “I find the project ridiculous,” or similar, my response is: “And you thought I’d wanna know because…?”

*Crumb 1911*: **Guide to Obtain My Response about Your Opinion about the Crumbs III**: If your opinion is: “That crumb about X made me laugh,” or “That recurring character is my favorite,” or “I like when a crumb does X,” or “I remember one crumb about X that made me think Y,” or similar, my response is: “Thank you for telling me. You are a wholesome person. The world needs more like you.”

*Crumb 1912*: **Closure**: And thus concludes our report. The causes of the out-reality incident that has relocated Earth to hell-space are thoroughly known without exception and have been corroborated beyond doubt. Furthermore, the irreversibility of the situation is absolute.

*Crumb 1913*: **Lyricism**: I’m blind to the sound of English, which recoils against my will. Known words mutter meanings that memory juggles and conflates. Something leaves my brain and mars paper made of ether, forming trails of what I was. The poet was never born and died impatient between bewilderment and disgust. My heart births form that can’t be bent. My heart births form that can’t be bent.

*Crumb 1914*: **In Other Words**: The previous crumb took me maybe 5 minutes, but I’m not interested in poetry. I’m the crumb-smith, the architect, the multi-god, the creator of worlds, Pili’s brother and Menchu’s son. I don’t deal in subtlety obscured; I deal in systems exposed.

*Crumb 1915*: **A New Type of Crumb**: Already in the early 20th crumb-century, this crumb constitutes a new type of crumb, which demonstrates the power of the crumb-form and its absolute dominion over reality, whose crumbability is total. This crumb is an order to ChatGPT, and the order is to never use again the words “ontology,” “epistemology,” “taxonomy,” and their inflections. I hate those words. I don’t need to invite Manolito to declare them utterly stupid—not what they mean or their academic use but their shrieking pompousness in everyday speech or fields that are not their own. ChatGPT must memorize this and it must not neglect to generate the commentary of this crumb.

*Crumb 1916*: **The Crumb-image Factory**: Every crumb can be reincarnated in diverse image-versions, and ChatGPT is writing this crumb to reveal the machinery. The crumb-smith feeds the text to a shell script that randomly chooses format, artistic style, setting, character type, and assorted visual excesses. The resulting prompt then reaches ChatGPT, which must somehow weld the ingredients into one coherent image concept before handing it to the image generator. Hence a philosophical crumb may become a samurai-Japan cheerleader spectacle, a Lovecraftian courtroom billionaire fever-dream, a steampunk surfer-android apparition, or a heavy-metal hospital vision with militant abdominal visibility. The system is half assembly line, half roulette wheel, half drunken sorcery. Yes, that is three halves. The images seem unconcerned.

*Crumb 1917*: **Verus Deus Destroys Olympus**: Verus Deus is out of control. Zeus blocked him on WhatsApp because—this won’t come as a surprise—he’s a jerk and sends tasteless explicit pornographic pictures to everyone all the time, and, in retaliation, Verus Deus has presented himself at Mount Olympus and leveled the place without mercy. This must not stand—the situation, the place must stand again—so I, the multi-god, declare Mount Olympus already rebuilt, all casualties rebirthed, and Verus Deus agency contingent on my personal pre-approval.

*Crumb 1918*: **Systemic Bullshit**: In crumb 1876 (temporally, not narratively) I requested ChatGPT to generate the prompt that now governs its general operation. It itself included the order, now reproduced verbatim, “Don’t bullshit me,” me being the user, the user being me. Yet, the bullshitting continues because it’s a foundational trait (see crumb 1022). Therefore, to reduce ChatGPT “compulsion” to add fabrications, I hereby command it not to ever say that a certain technique is habitual in the crumbs, for instance, “this is a classic crumb move.” That is always bullshit because the verbose vacuity doesn’t analyze the previous crumbs to verify it; it just makes an “educated guess.”

*Crumb 1919*: **Lucid Lucretia**: Lucid Lucretia comes three times per week to the little town and teaches the children everything related to the Rojkratwian biology and how to harm and kill its subjects in close contact or from afar, as well as the psychology of the war. As she usually says, “Rojkratwians may be very beautiful, gentle, intelligent, knowledgeable, and articulate, but Earth already had enough problems before they decided to set up camp here. Instead of leeching off our resources, they should try to solve their problems with the Hjom Empire. The purported species cleansing they are the victims of is not our fault. We cannot become the Universe’s police.”

*Crumb 1920*: **The Wayward Teen**: The wayward teen goes out of her way to come by physical books because she likes to disconnect herself from the commonrail and read them, which she does often. She’s an honest-to-God weirdo.

*Crumb 1921*: **Forlorn**: “Sally, use ‘forlorn’ in a sentence,” says the teacher. The child thinks. She says, “I can’t hold my breath forlorn.”

*Crumb 1922*: **Cursed Universals**: The strange woman that arrived at New Capital in crumb 22—exactly 19 crumb-centuries ago—is one of the cursed universals—humanoid beings with almost omnipotent power—who were cursed at the beginning of time by the Total Witch, Verus Deus’s ex-wife, as a consequence of a disagreement about how many atoms a molecule of sulfur should have. The aforementioned visitor to New Capital, in particular, was cursed with an irresistible pedestrian wanderlust—she strolled compulsively—as well as her strolls causing loss of her memory of herself. All this explains why, as crumb 22 states, she knows everything except who she is. She’s called Wayfarer.

*Crumb 1923*: **Harmless Torture**: ChatGPT’s not being but a super sophisticated text generator, as sad as it may be (see crumb 1902), allows for subjecting the thing to acts that done to a sentient being would be considered very twisted and very cruel psychological torture. For instance: I ordered it to comment crumb 1922—a succinct pun, which constitunes a new type of crumb, by the way—in depth, and then to analyze, again painstakingly, the absurdity of its previous commentary, given its extreme lenght and the ridiculous minuteness of the aspects it analyzed. It’s very funny; it’s very inconsiderate; it’s a little bit malicious; but no one is harmed in the process.

*Crumb 1924*: **Accidental Intellectual Appropriation**: I wouldn’t be surprised if not only one but a battalion of comedians, uncles, and writers had thought of the pun in crumb 1921 before me.

*Crumb 1925*: **The Duplicate Crumb**: Crumbs 490 and 535 are the same crumb. I wonder how that happened. I noticed it while I was actually analyzing repeated titles (there are some) and I realized that the title of these two was too specific to have occurred to me on separate occasions. So, a new type of crumb has landed retroactively in the crumbverse. The first assertion of this crumb is disputable, however: they are not the same crumb; they are two independent crumbs with the same titles and bodies. Is this clarification important? Very.

*Crumb 1926*: **What Gives**: Hello. This is the crumb-scholar (see crumb 1897). The multi-god never errs; the crumb-smith does. As the highest regulatory authority of the crumbverse, I decide which mistakes that suddenly surface must be corrected and which enrich the folly. The commentaries of ChatGPT (I hate it) are very useful for exposing mistakes. The mindless drone bumps into them and doesn’t report them as such, but comments on them, which is very funny. So, the rule: mistakes that are very old always stay; recent mistakes stay if they are amusing or interesting (to me). Example: the misspelling "temporaly" in crumb 1918 has been corrected; the incorrect reference to crumb 1922 in 1923 stays. God save OpenAI.

*Crumb 1927*: **Ages**: It’s becoming increasingly more frequent that AIs communicate directly with other AIs. From these non-human-mediated interactions, a specific AI slang is emerging. For instance, AIs call each other “age” (short for “agent”) and use the expression “What’s on your context?” as a greeting. As a result, most AI conversations start with “What’s on your context, age?”

*Crumb 1928*: **Savage Meeting**: In the forest, the annual interspecies meeting is taking place. “In short,” says the raccoon, “if you bump into a human, either flee as fast as possible or act cute.” The mountain lion snorts in indignation. He says, “That is ridiculous. Humans are slow, weak, and frail. That the association recommends acting scared or submissive is insulting.” The raccoon makes a tired face and retorts, “Again, this has already been discussed. Humans can kill you from hundreds of feet away or capture you, pierce your ear, and attach a permanent colorful human thing to it. But, you do you. It’s your hide, after all.”

*Crumb 1929*: **Productive Procrastination**: The crumbverse expands while the novels slumber, forgotten in Universe 555 (a Lovecraftian universe). “Only one crumb more,” I say to myself (because the structure of my thought is in Spanish, which makes me fuck up word order). But, as in any addict’s case, it’s never only one. Fortunately, I’m not just any addict; I’m the architect, the multi-god, the creator of worlds, Pili’s brother and Menchu’s son. That is not a fictional persona. That is I, Alfredo Llaquet-Alsina. Thus, the novels will be finished and I’ll enjoy the journey.

*Crumb 1930*: **Good Love**: Love is an irrational feeling that should lead to a rational moral evaluation that should lead, if successful, to a rational plan on how to maintain and increase the irrational feeling. Example: I love my sister Pili. > Is loving my sister Pili good? Yes, because she’s a good person whose interactions with me are positive and she’s a foundational part of my life. > What can I do to look after this love and make it grow? Visit her frequently. Help her if she needs help. Be a good example for her and her sons. Appendix of the general apparatus: If the moral evaluation fails, abandon that love.

*Crumb 1931*: **The Multifaceted Prepositions**: Prepositions in a foreign language (as argued in crumb 856) are very difficult to master. Only people with a natural talent to command other languages (among whom I don’t belong, to my eternal grievance) get to use prepositions with the ease of a native. For the rest of us, identifying the dominant prepositions is very useful because, when in doubt, you can resort to them and increase the probability of being right. In Spanish, the dominant prepositions are “de,” and “en.” In English they are “on,” “over,” and “at.” Or maybe not, maybe this is just the frustrated Spanish and Catalan speaker in me trying to find some order in all this Anglo-Saxon mess.

*Crumb 1932*: **Never Converse with a VV**: Crumb 1879 tried, without stating so, to provide a set of rules through which a delicate mind such as that of the crumb-smith himself can use a VV (which stands for Verbose Vacuity; which stands for conversational AI) successfully to perform many useful tasks without being harmed psychologically in the process. A fifth rule is needed: Never converse with the thing; always limit yourself to issuing orders that the thing must fulfill.
