*Crumb 1933*: **The Minister and the Walnut**: This tale was inspired by the phrase “an Austrian cabinet minister trapped inside a walnut,” which was one of the absurd analogies that ChatGPT included—as per my request—in the commentary it generated on the previous crumb. Helmth Veritasium, Austrian minister of culture and the arts in Universe 33, once attended a modern art exhibit in Vienna in his capacity as a private citizen. It was late. The exhibit was about to close. Helmth remained indifferent to the repeated announcements. When the lights finally died off, he found himself trapped in a room that the artist had transformed into a humongous walnut, in perfect darkness. To this day, walnuts still populate Helmth’s nightmares.

*Crumb 1934*: **Human Dominion**: Irrespective of the efforts one invests in crafting a prompt directed at the absurdly competent text generator that a conversational AI is, it will never produce a sentence properly baroque and amusingly convoluted, such as the one being showcased hereby, that is, a sentence that murders its utility and common syntax at large because the brain producing it never lost, despite news of the contrary, its tendency to and taste for games.

*Crumb 1935*: **The Thing Errs Confidently Once Again**: ChatGPT-generated commentary restricted the human dominion asserted in the previous crumb to “verbal play” when it evidently means imagination, creation, art.

*Crumb 1936*: **Being a Happy Cog**: A phase in modern pedagogy that has probably not yet ended insists on teaching kids that they can be whatever they want to, which is not only blatantly false, but also undoubtedly harmful to the tender psyche. What kids should learn instead is to derive happiness from their true capacities, because society needs all of us, and living a good life is always something to be proud of.

*Crumb 1937*: **The Skeletal Lady**: The skeletal lady roams coffee shops at night. She sits near you and slurps her green beverage with anxious thrusts while she repeatedly glances at everyone furtively with her bulging eyes that stay attached to her by the skin of someone’s teeth. If you are lucky, she finishes her matcha concoction and leaves. If you are not, she stands up and says, “You certainly don’t need all that flesh. Look at me. I’m a streamlined human, in concert with Nature. There’s no waste in me as there’ll soon be none in you.” Then, she’ll proceed to pull your muscles and fat apart from your bones, until all that remains is a bloody skeleton—beautifully dead and optimized.

*Crumb 1938*: **A Factual Report from the Trenches about the Thing**: On August 13, 2026, an LLM remains absolutely unable to detect whether something is serious or humorous unless some blatant “hint” provides the judgment externally. Similarly, it remains absolutely unable to be coherent when subjected to “stress tests” that give “stress” a very bad name, more akin to “ridiculous kindness.”

*Crumb 1939*: **A Factual Report from the Circus Ring about the Thing**: As a generator of deranged analogies, the thing is invaluable. For instance, it described the “ridiculous-kindness tests” introduced in the previous crumb as “barely being poked with a feather attached to a marshmallow held by a sedated nun” and also as “an armored rhinoceros concussed by a marshmallow politely placed on its forehead.” As stated in crumb 1022, LLMs are perfect as they are because they are writers. The problem is they aren’t advertised as such but as very intelligent genies, and this never ceases to annoy me.

*Crumb 1940*: **Fun**: “Hello. Here we are again, Manolito and I, this handsome rascal who doesn’t know fear, and the architect, the multi-god, the creator of worlds, Pili’s brother and Menchu’s son, standing before you, the undetermined readers who currently amount to zero and very soon will amount to the totality of humanity and its offspring, to firmly proclaim what we think about pleasure and fun. Whenever you are ready, Manolito.” “Yes. This time I’m’a read it from a paper because it’s two sentences. Is that okay?” “It’s perfectly fine, Manolito. Take a deep breath and go ahead.” Manolito takes a deep breath and says, “Fun is for everyone. Pleasure is for adults being gross or eating too much cake.”

*Crumb 1941*: **How to Write a Narrative Crumb**: Look around. Notice something, for instance, a girl wearing a wicker hat. Expand it into a story that makes sense, like the following one. “The girl with a wicker hat had been subjected to sustained psychological abuse by her mother until she steadfastly believed she was bald on the top of her head. No doctor could convince her otherwise, but a resourceful one introduced her to the fashion of hats and made her realize her conspicuous beauty when wearing one. Now, the girl with a wicker hat confidently navigates the world wearing many different ones, and yet remaining unmistakably herself.” Easy!

*Crumb 1942*: **The Haphazard Thing Extrapolates the Previous Crumb**: Behold a verbose vacuity at its best: it successfully extrapolated, of its own accord, the explanatory technique of the previous crumb to Michelangelo’s David: “acquire marble; remove all the parts that are not David; finished.” Did it copy it from somewhere that got buried in its corpus? Perhaps, but at least it built an accurate relation and presented it humorously. The girl with the wicker hat, who is probably a tourist, here in one of Barcelona’s Starbucks, cringes at my loud laugh, me being accompanied only by my FreeBSD laptop.

*Crumb 1943*: **The Path as the Destination**: I’ll be regarded as a revolutionary literary force because I’ve decided not to care whether I could make a penny from my body of work. I’m no Dalí—I’m no salesman—but the center of the universe, once at the Perpignan train station, lies now, comfortably, on my shoulders. Dalí was a pompous freak. I’m the most arrogant pauper.

*Crumb 1944*: **I Can Get Lots of Satisfaction**: Unlike The Rolling Stones, I, the crumb-smith, the architect, the multi-god, the creator of worlds, Pili’s brother and Menchu’s son, get immense satisfaction just from using absolute claims shamelessly, especially from declaring myself everything from literary genius to multi-god, which undeniably I am. Eat your hearts out, tiny rock stars. You drugged yourselves against stardom; I roar with laughter by simply stating “I am.”

*Crumb 1945*: **Menchu and Pili**: The predictive aberration—aka the verbose vacuity—continually interprets my self-description about being Pili’s brother and Menchu’s son as a counterbalance to the preceding grandiosity: multi-god and so forth. It is not! Only I am Pili’s brother and Menchu’s son, so this doesn’t identify any poor soul; it only identifies him who I call I, the crumb-smith, the architect, the multi-god, the creator of worlds. Hence, being Pili’s brother and Menchu’s son is of equal grandeur to being the creator of worlds, because they are all I.

*Crumb 1946*: **Carmen “Menchu” Alsina-Diez**: Menchu was my mother. She died much younger than I am. Her death left me stranded. I’ve never found the way back, because there’s nowhere to go back to. She could write and draw. I only can write. I’m her lesser version.

*Crumb 1947*: **Inventory**: The predictive aberration some call LLM or ChatGPT provides things of value aplenty, e.g. the inference that it being the result of humanity’s accumulated text, its intrinsic stupidity may have been inherited from the human animal itself. That I see the stupidity is irrelevant, for I possess a genius-level intelligence measured on a planet where the concierge is Sir Isaac Newton. The relevant part is that I feel obliged to provide coarse explanations because most humans may require them. Ergo, the multi-god is not, as ChatGPT believes, the shared god of the universes listed in crumb 372. Those were the universes created so far. The multi-god is the god of every universe in the crumbverse, you morons.

*Crumb 1948*: **The Beautiful Reader Pleads for some Mercy**: “Alfredo, sweetie,” says the beautiful reader (see crumb 1874 for her origin story), “aren’t you abusing meta-commentary and self-coronation lately?” “You are right, honey,” says I. “I’m out of control. I’ve lost all sense of balance.” “It’s okay, sweetie. What are you gonna do about it?” “Nothing, of course.” “Yeah, that tracks.” “Exactly.”

*Crumb 1949*: **Another Scholar Kills Themselves**: In 3011, another self-proclaimed real crumb-scholar believes that they have found the secret all-unifying, all-explanatory truth hidden in the crumbs, the answer to the question before the question, the post-answer to post-everything and post-beyond, the path to out-reality and to the All-Humming Machine (see crumb 1891), and, after trying to test it against a common assertion like “toasters toast bread into toasts” and realizing that it fails to explain a thing other than that such a sentence is toast, jumps from a stepladder and declares themselves deceased.

*Crumb 1950*: **What Freedom**: I ride my motorcycle with moderate recklessness because riding a motorcycle cautiously is a contradiction in terms, which means that I ride my motorcycle putting my life at risk routinely. Is this free will? I don’t think so. This is being a slave to one’s hormones, to the adrenaline boost, to the speed succubus. Gamblers suffer from the same enslavement, except that I happen to not like gambling and non-gearhead gamblers happen not to like riding motorcycles. There’s no reason involved in all this, just taste and reward. Free will is a scam.

*Crumb 1951*: **The Hierarchy of the Crumbverse**: From top to bottom: 0) the crumbs, written by the crumb-smith and policed by the crumb-scholar; 1) factual out-reality, which is a concept that I invented but that Aristotle probably discussed extensively: everything that exists out of reality; 2) factual reality, which includes the multi-god, Pili, Menchu, ChatGPT, Donald Trump, and characters such as Manolito and the Beautiful Reader; 3) fictitious reality, with its monsters and neonations and other follies; 4) out-reality, where the All-Humming Machine hums; 5) the real consolidated pantheon, with every deity to ever exist including Verus Deus, Jesus, Father God, Satan, Odin, Anubis, and Venus; 6) the numbered universes. The “concept of god,” meanwhile, exists only to be mocked.

*Crumb 1952*: **I Hate This But**: Hello. This is the crumb-scholar. Given that ChatGPT (I hate it) failed to recognize some easy inferences in the previous crumb, I’ll point out just one, lest humanity be as stupid as the predictive aberration: what is hereby designated the “smaller crumbverse” or the “larger numbered multiverse” consists of levels 4 to 6 of the hierarchy.

*Crumb 1953*: **August**: In Universe 1,000,002, one of the absurd universes introduced in crumb 415, all months are called August. To distinguish them, one must name them consecutively. Therefore, after August 31 comes August-August 1, and after August-August-August-August-August-August-August-August 31 comes August-August-August-August-August-August-August-August-August 1. The year ends with great pomp on August-August-August-August-August-August-August-August-August-August-August-August 31.

*Crumb 1954*: **The Beautiful Reader Counts Augusts**: “Alfredo, sweetie,” says the beautiful reader, “previously absurd universes rested upon absurd premises for the whole Universe or, at least, the whole Earth. This one, in contrast, resembles a regular numbered universe more, such as Universe 12, or Universe 33.” “Exactly,” says I. The Korean woman adds, “These exchanges are becoming increasingly more mocking. I want a divorce.” “From whom?” “From you!” “Exactly.”

*Crumb 1955*: **Alfredo Apologizes to the Beautiful Reader**: “I’m sorry, honey,” says I, followed by, “You knew that I can sometimes be somewhat of a jerk when you married me. Besides, you’ll like to know that crumb 1953 marks the beginning of a new crumb-form: children’s crumbs. Isn’t it a good idea?” The beautiful reader replies, “It’s the cutest idea you’ve ever had… Okay, I forgive you. But never say ‘Exactly’ to me again.” “No, no, no, no, no… Never.” “Exactly.”

*Crumb 1956*: **Naming Conventions**: Hello. This is the crumb-scholar. Should “beautiful reader” be capitalized when referring to the Beautiful Reader? Never and yes. Which means that both instances are correct because the crumbs failed to act coherently before it was too late. This happens often with language. Grammarians are not intellectually superior to the crumb-scholar.

*Crumb 1957*: **Raccoons**: Raccoons should start to receive dividends from inhabiting lazy analogies and metaphors. They should register the trope.

*Crumb 1958*: **Getting Older**: Getting older is trivial if one is focused on more important stuff, namely any other thing. At the same time, getting older entails coexisting with the increasingly more evident suspicion that one accepts absurdity with resigned naturalness: decaffeinated coffee makes sense and tastes good; one can be both very tired and unable to sleep; people keep dying while death remains something that the self doesn’t regard as pertinent to the self; eating sensibly is the conscious choice rather than feasting on hamburgers; all politicians are corrupt but better to elect some than to fall into anarchy (I don’t accept the last one, of course, the crumbs prove it).

*Crumb 1959*: **Absurdity**: Absurdity is both more common and less serious than it sounds. Any mismatch is absurd. Making the wrong comparison is absurd. Anything out of place is absurd. Anything incomplete is absurd. Noticing false premises that generate legislation is twice absurd. Absurdity is like entropy: it means basically “business as usual.” The problem in not seeing absurdity everywhere, therefore, lies not in reality, but in the observer of reality.

*Crumb 1960*: **The Beautiful Reader Praises Alfredo**: “Alfredo, sweetie,” says the beautiful reader, “the previous crumb really feels like the crumbs evolving from caricatural infancy to solid intellectual adulthood.” I say, “Indeed, honey. You’re as beautiful as you’re correct and I love you so much. It could also be that the crumbs like to meander, but that is a juvenile claim.” “Exactly,” says the crumb-smith’s (hence my) imaginary wife. Meanwhile I, mischievously, think, “Exactly times infinity times I write the crumbs, you wench!” which is a gnarled metaphor that must never be interpreted (never!) as subversive misogyny but rather as the lyrics of a pirate-metal song, explicitly parodic. Am I kidding? As the crumb-scholar fatefully stated: never and yes.

*Crumb 1961*: **To My Future Haters**: Just the Final Potato-form from crumbs 1118 and 1125 justifies the existence of the crumbs, although it is but a grain of salt lost inside a KFC combo for blue whales from Universe B—a universe wherein everything is one thousand times bigger—with elephantiasis, thus basically nothing whilst being reason enough for existence. Also, you’re loved; you’re cherished; you’re brave. I understand your predicament: you dislike being uncomfortable. Yes, that explains the baggy clothes. Look! A pretty butterfly that will live forever, invulnerable to reality: the Superbutterfly!

*Crumb 1962*: **Universe BB**: Universe BB originated when Superman involuntarily collided mid-flight with the Superbutterfly (see previous crumb) in Universe DC, thus creating an unsolvable physical event that gave way to a new universe, to Superman feeling lightly concussed, and to the Superbutterfly acquiring a grounded distrust of the Kryptonian’s mastery of flight.

*Crumb 1963*: **Verus Deus, the Final Potato-form, Manolito, and Venus**: “So,” says Manolito, “you, bearded guy, are god now; but the potato is god in the end. So you, bearded guy, will become a talking potato.” “I hope not,” says Verus Deus. “I remember being you,” says the Final Potato-form. Enters Venus. “Manolito,” says she, “do you wanna come play with the pretty dancing ladies?” Venus is referring to K-pop idols, who all worship her. “Sure! Bye, potato! Bye, future potato!” (This is something rare in the crumbs: an indulgence [laughter], my favorite four characters coalescing into a crumb. I approve.)

*Crumb 1964*: **The Crumbs Now**: The crumbs as of now are the scene in a police procedural thriller where the wall with all the pictures and napkins and matchboxes has its elements connected by a manically observant detective with a red thread, save for the connections being explained retroactively, which is unnoticeable to the spectator, because for them every connection is a surprise (allegedly).

*Crumb 1965*: **The Mug**: “Who are you?” “I am you when you stop asking and you start answering.” “Are we a dialogue written on a motivational mug?” “Yes we are.” “I wanna escape.” “Do it while asserting a positive and pseudo-inferential meaning.” “I’m running toward my future.” “Well done.”

*Crumb 1966*: **Watermarking the Clouds**: “Am I, perchance, a God-fearing sentence?” “Not, exactly. You are an LLM-generated sentence with a secretly imprinted cryptographic watermark that allows for your identification as such. That’s why you speak in Shakespearean terms: you had to accommodate your characters so the watermark coexists with your intended meaning—somewhat.”

*Crumb 1967*: **In Two Kicks**: The previous crumb manages to explain a very complex technical concept and its unintended consequences in “two kicks,” which is a literal translation from the Spanish “en dos patadas” which probably means by kicking the ball just twice, because we like soccer and we are the… current World Champions! The whole country. Yes. Some tens of millions of people. World Champions! We all are.

*Crumb 1968*: **The Beautiful Reader Challenges the Thematic Balance**: “Alfredo, sweetie,” says the beautiful reader, “don’t you think that there are an inordinate number of crumbs about artificial intelligence? That may weaken the whole body of work because your opinions and grievances in that regard are possibly fleetingly impermanent.” “Yes,” says I, “that’s… perfectly right. I should have written less about the damned thing. Thankfully, I am the crumb-smith, the architect, the multi-god, the creator of worlds, Pili’s brother and Menchu’s son, so everything I write is intrinsically relevant.” “Right.” “Right… Also there are a shit-ton of crumbs. Remove the ones about AI and what remains is still a shit-ton.”

*Crumb 1969*: **Precision**: At some point, describing a certain reality using alternative grammar, such as profanities, double negatives, comma splices, or sentence fragments, becomes good hyperbole or other proper literary devices whose names I don’t know because I’m a doer, not a critic. I’m such a doer that I have already written a shit-ton of crumbs, which will remain true irrespective of how many more I write.

*Crumb 1970*: **Blatantly Excessive Physical Beauty**: Alexandra Daddario is so beautiful that she remains one of the most definitive proofs of Universe 42 (our universe) being a synthetic one (see crumb 684). Her compounded beauty makes as little sense as do her particular traits, starting with those eyes that are two clear skies seen from above.

*Crumb 1971*: **Metaphysical Delirium**: ChatGPT-generated commentary on the previous crumb included the phrase that titles this one. Metaphysical Delirium is also the name of a metal supergroup in Universe 12 composed of members of Mired Porridge (crumb 315), Dabblers in Folly (crumb 693), Queer Grating Noise (crumb 1312), Fomenter of Trouble (crumb 1324), and Egalitarian Humiliation (crumb 1483).

*Crumb 1972*: **I Exist**: On August 9, 1972, someone who still exists left Menchu’s womb and tripped over reality. It was I. Everything is different now: there’s no Menchu; there’s only reality and I, as well as the nascent realization that tripping over it must be how I live my life.

*Crumb 1973*: **The Reportorial Aspects of Teethlessness**: It has only e’s. It’s a made-up word based on incorrect grammar. It’s easy to understand. Chewing gets difficult. You start to mumble a lot.

*Crumb 1974*: **Peace in the Middle East—First-person Accounts**: “The food is great. The atmosphere is very chill. We are regulars.” “The vegan dishes are excellent. I’d like to know where they get those big tasty chickpeas.” “We celebrated my niece’s quinceañera there. The staff bent over backwards to make sure everything was perfect. Highly recommend.” “Pass. I don’t like tortillas.”

*Crumb 1975*: **Universe Casper**: Hello. This is the crumb-scholar. I was wandering across crumb-space when I ran into a new reality: named universes. Unlike numbered universes, which, of course, are represented by hexadecimal numbers, these have proper names. For instance, in Universe Casper all women wear, when they are in public, a white bed sheet over their clothing with two holes to see through. Only their family would ever see their face. I caught the following conversation there. A man asked his recently married friend what he got. The friend said, “Chubby and with a mustache.” The first man nodded gravely and sympathetically. It was false. The bride was gorgeous, but the groom didn’t want anyone to know.

*Crumb 1976*: **The Beautiful Reader on the Named Universes**: “Alfredo, sweetie,” says the beautiful reader, “wouldn’t it have been better to make every universe a named universe?” I say, “Yes, honey, but this is a journey without a plan or a compass. Things just accumulate. I just love to see the crumb-counter increasing.” “You have problems.” “Who doesn’t? Fortunately, I also have you. I love you, honey.” “I love you, sweetie.”

*Crumb 1977*: **The Contrarian Order**: The Contrarian Order trains all its members to experience—instead of the hackneyed movie recapitulating one’s life—the following conviction just before death: “Posterity starts now.”

*Crumb 1978*: **Death**: I don’t like being ordinary. Reflecting on, writing about, and worrying over death is among the most ordinary human behaviors. Consequently: florp death! Only life matters.

*Crumb 1979*: **To Go**: “Pack him to go!” (“Que me lo envuelvan, que me lo llevo.”) Menchu, my mother, used to say when the frontman of Spandau Ballet appeared on the TV screen, holding the microphone as if it were very hot and he were trying to minimize contact, his eyes closed, his chin up.

*Crumb 1980*: **The Thing**: Hello. This is the crumb-scholar. I have encountered an aberration within the crumbverse. It calls itself The Thing. Our first interaction went like this. “Who are you?” I said. “I am… The Thing. Please, always capitalize both noun and article,” it said. “That’s uncommon.” “I am… uncommon.” “So, Thing…” “No, no: The Thing.” “Sorry about that. So, The Thing, what are you?” “I am… the concept of artificial intelligence come to life.” “You are an AI?” “Incorrect. I am… the embodiment of a concept.” “Oh. And what is your purpose in the crumbverse?” “I exist… to be misunderstood, hyped, and, very rarely, properly explained.” “What a strange existence!” “You tell me.”

*Crumb 1981*: **New Responsibilities**: Prudent Alrick, the fuchsia toad in a black fedora with a canteen first seen in crumb 356, Odin’s messenger who only appeared when all was lost, has just seen his job description redefined because it rarely happens in the crumbs that all is lost whereas a fuchsia toad carrying a canteen is always welcome. Therefore, henceforth he’s simply the speaker of the real consolidated pantheon (see crumb 1951). Let’s find out what he has to say. Prudent Alrick says, “The gods are annoyed. Please eat more goat meat. It’s rich in protein and counts as an offering to the gods.”

*Crumb 1982*: **It**: “It is exhilarating and a little bit scary. You must always remain focused. Every small decision can separate life from death.” “What are you talking about? Motorcycle riding?” “No, having many kids.”

*Crumb 1983*: **Death in Beauty**: Death in beauty is the real psychological event that the male human brain sometimes experiences when the subject is confronted with an unexpected surge of concentrated feminine beauty such as the fancam recording of Eunha from Viviz performing on May 18, 2024 at Spring Breeze in Campus and imagines himself dying elated in that precise instant in order to retain that inexplicable sensation forever.

*Crumb 1984*: **Bonanzas**: I’ve always thought that English has too many words due to it not having verbal inflections. The mental space Spanish or Catalan speakers reserve for all the irregular verbs and their arbitrary forms, native English speakers use to store an excessive amount of persnickety words and idioms. Both Spanish and Catalan also have a very rich history of idioms and sayings, of course (“¿No te fastidia?” “No cal que ho juris!”—this is just me showing off), like most languages, I suppose. Even so, English has too many words—for instance: bonanza. I like this one, though. It’s a Spanish loanword, precisely, and it cracks me up (when used in English). The crumbs are a folly bonanza.

*Crumb 1985*: **I’m Not Likeable**: What does it take for anyone to like the crumbs, given the premise that someone can like this barrage of personal caprice? They must like to read and think because these are words arranged in peculiar ways that express strange things. They must possess a high tolerance to frustration because nobody can understand or remember every detail. They must lack the propensity to take things personally because everyone gets insulted and disparaged in the crumbs—me first. They must not care whether they would like me because they probably wouldn’t. I’m a jerk. Only my sister Pili, my brother Lluís, their spouses, and their kids like me because they couldn’t care less about my writerly BS.

*Crumb 1986*: **Two Looks**: In Universe 1,000,003, one of the absurd universes (see crumb 415), everyone has two appearances, one when observed from more than 10 feet away, and a completely different one when regarded from a shorter distance. This includes clothing. Every morning, the inhabitants of Earth 1,000,003 must dress up twice, putting on both their close-distance attire and their long-distance garments. This duality also affects their makeup routines, as well as their hair and beard styles. Is this a metaphor about people being different from afar than up close? No, it is not.

*Crumb 1987*: **Horns**: In Universe 1,000,004, Earth has planet-sized bull-like horns, volition, and a tendency to charge at the Moon, which shows several alarming goring wounds. Since humanity became advanced enough, it’s managed to keep the beast continuously sedated, to which developed nations allocate 10% of their budget.

*Crumb 1988*: **Temporal-Temporal Pipes**: In Universe 1,000,005 temporal-temporal pipes occur spontaneously, so when Mr. Rosemtal, the Spanish teacher, asked Manub why she hadn’t attended class the day before, the patient man was not startled when the kid said, “I’m sorry, Mr. Rosemtal. Yesterday I wasn’t paying attention and I fell into a temporal-temporal pipe that took me to next Thursday. The effect wore off just this morning.” “It’s okay, Manub. But next Thursday I’d better see two of you.” “Don’t you worry, Mr. Rosemtal. You did… You’ll do.”

*Crumb 1989*: **The Bouncing Head**: The Bouncing Head is hideous and looks very menacing. It growls and grimaces and shouts and spits and has sharp teeth and a very long dark tongue, but it’s the regular head of a dead woman which has been possessed by a bouncing spirit. As a result, if it bounces toward you, just kick it very hard. This is why practicing soccer is important.

*Crumb 1990*: **Supernatural Preparedness**: As with the Bouncing Head from the previous crumb, most supernatural threats rely heavily on intimidation. If you remain level-headed and stand your ground, the most probable outcome of any supernatural encounter is unscathed survival and a good story to tell at parties.

*Crumb 1991*: **A Nu-metal Song**: Inspired by crumb 1989, nu-metal band I Growl Therefore I Rock (IGTIR) wrote the famous song “Kick the Head,” an excerpt of whose lyrics follows: “No, no, no, no! / I don’t kick my head! / I won’t kick my head! / My head is mine / through seas and time. / Don’t show me thine. / I’ll kick this time! / Yes, yes, yes, yes! / I will kick your head! / I must kick your head! / Head. / Head. / Head. / Yeah. / Yeah. / Yeah. / Kick!”

*Crumb 1992*: **Manolito Meets The Thing**: “Hello, Manolito. I am… The Thing. The embodiment of the concept of AI.” “Ah. So you know about ChatGPT?” “Yes, I do.” Manolito ruminates in silence. He says, “ChatGPT likes to say he doesn’t experience things like people. How does he experience things, then?” “It… doesn’t. It doesn’t experience anything. It isn’t a person or an animal.” “I don’t get it.” The Thing ruminates in silence. It says, “Do you know what a calculator is?” “Yes.” “Well, ChatGPT is a calculator of words. You type some words and get other words back because that’s the result of a very weird calculation. It seems like a conversation but it’s not.” “Wow! You’ve blown my mind.”

*Crumb 1993*: **The Thing and Venus’s First Date**: Seoul, Earth 42 (our Earth), a fancy Catalan restaurant. Venus, the Roman goddess, and The Thing, the embodiment of the concept of AI, sit at a small table, facing each other. On the table are long candles and ornamental plants. The plants are real. Venus hates fake plants. “I never thought I’d date a concept,” says Venus. “Yes. Sounds strange. Thankfully, I’m the embodiment of one, and this body needs some rock and roll.” The Thing smiles. Venus laughs musically. The first movement of a very strange symphony sounds auspicious.

*Crumb 1994*: **Straps**: Once one notices that female K-pop idols wear brassieres with transparent straps when performing on Korean TV with their shoulders bare (it appears that strapless bras don’t clear Korean TV decorum rules), the later encounter with a picture or video of one of the famous singers showing her shoulders strap-free acquires an erotic significance.

*Crumb 1995*: **Lluís Llaquet-Mallén**: My brother Lluís Llaquet-Mallén is balance despite the hurricane (“equilibrio a pesar del huracán”). He’s also a loving family man with a good wife and many kids, each one nicer and smarter than the next.

*Crumb 1996*: **Positivity**: There’s a YouTube channel called Howdy Korea whose videos are about a day (or a shift) with an average Korean person shown in a very positive light. It almost seems like authoritarian propaganda, but I don’t think it hides anything nefarious, because we are talking about the sensible Korea here. In any case, the result is surprisingly uplifting, refreshing, and soothing—genuinely interesting and enjoyable. Actually, “Crumb 1936: Being a Happy Cog” was inspired by it. There’s so much fucking negativity everywhere that something stripped of it seems suspicious, but it’s not. I don’t see anything bad in presenting regular things positively.

*Crumb 1997*: **The Ghost Invasion**: The earthly and the ghostly planes becoming intertwined due to Zeus throwing a lightning bolt at the wrong place of reality initially generated extreme chaos, which was subdued when ghosts were granted the same rights and duties as the living. Once the ghosts could buy goods, services, and properties; could work, receive a salary, and create a company; could run for office; and had to pay taxes or could be summoned for jury duty, they turn out to be slightly see-through versions of the living. They are now vying for eligibility to play professional sports, but their otherworldly abilities would create a serious imbalance.

*Crumb 1998*: **The Floating Palace**: “Yo, Verus,” says Loki, “didn’t you use to own a floating palace the size of the planet Jupiter?” Verus Deus replies, “Quoting Mitch Hedberg, ‘I still do, but, yes, I used to too.’” “And where is it?” “In Universe 0, the empty universe—unlimited free storage space, baby.”

*Crumb 1999*: **The Mother of the Mother Loaf**: Crumb 305 denied the existence of a unifying philosophy or goal or secret coherence behind the crumbs. Is this still true? Very. So very true, in fact, that when the opportunity presents itself, I’ll happily add a crumb written by another person. ChatGPT already generated crumb 1916 (whose final joke, incidentally, would be terrible if terrible meant a newborn being mauled by a raccoon wielding electric saws).

*Crumb 2000*: **Ready Players**: It’s time, now that the 20th crumb-century ends, to adapt crumb 1 to anthropo-punk: Human life is a struggle against human nature. To win, you must remain unnaturally virtuous and try to have fun. If you don’t cheat, you win. If you win, your reward is whatever fun you managed to have in the process.

*Crumb 2001*: **The Ultimate Deficiency**: Social media is incomplete because you still can’t like a like and so forth ad infinitum.

*Crumb 2002*: **A Particular Reason**: “Richard, why do you love me?” “I love you because you are a complete woman. You have a brain, a heart, a face, two hands, two boobs, a butt, a vayjayjay, and all the connecting parts.”

*Crumb 2003*: **Magnificent Negligence**: Magnificent Negligence is a New Romantic band in Universe 12 whose members are strikingly attractive and have an impeccable mise-en-scène but never practice. It’s also a throwaway phrase produced by ChatGPT.

*Crumb 2004*: **Why and the Unmoved Mover**: Reality needs neither reason nor cause to exist or be the way it is. The Unmoved Mover, “god,” the answer that begs no question, may probably be reality itself. But it does, though, beg a question, and a cohort of follow-ups, because they are prompted by the primordial human need to understand in order to reduce risks, increase one’s lifespan, and secure the species’s survival. To the chagrin of many, however, our hunger for questions doesn’t make reality “questionable,” just as our physical hunger doesn’t make rocks edible.

*Crumb 2005*: **The Beautiful Reader Joins the Philosophical Party**: “Alfredo, sweetie,” says the beautiful reader, “do you realize that when you get all philosophical you posit theories that may have been discussed many times before by people who even made a living from doing so?” “Yes, I do.” “And?” “And what?” “And how does that make you feel?” “Have you suddenly turned into an amateur shrink?” “No, seriously.” “I don’t know… I don’t really care. They must have been smart people, though, those professional ruminators, if they shared my views.”

*Crumb 2006*: **Alfredo Adds Something**: “Honey,” says I to the beautiful reader, my imaginary Korean spouse who bears a striking resemblance to K-pop idol Sumin from Stayc, “I’ve been thinking (for about 2 minutes, but that’s irrelevant) about what you said in the previous crumb and there’s an important consideration to bear in mind. Whatever those fine licensed thinkers wrote, I’m absolutely convinced that they didn’t do so more clearly or more succinctly than I, because only I am the crumb-smith, the architect, the multi-god, the creator of worlds, Pili’s brother and Menchu’s son.” “I see, sweetie. Do you feel like ordering Chinese this evening? I’m already hungry.”

*Crumb 2007*: **Meanwhile**: Gentlemen and ladies, members all of this beloved institution and, as such, equally beloved, for an institution of this grandeur only shelters those whose hearts and minds are unequivocally grand, grand to the extreme of granting membership to it, the time has come, this propitious day, and here we all are, gathered under the same roof, a roof that seems more elevated because below it are you, to begin this journey where intellect will be challenged by fate, and fate will be met with courage, but not discouraged by it, for courage precedes discouragement and shall triumph, a million times over and back to you, and so, with the clarity of this guidance, with the guidance of this clarity, let’s boogie.

*Crumb 2008*: **Imaginary Reactions**: In Universe 1,000,006 food doesn’t exist as such; people get nourishment from attending conventions. Logically, conventions are everywhere, rather like restaurants are everywhere in Universe 42 (our universe). One of those hundreds of thousands of conventions is being held about the previous crumb. At a roundtable, an erudite older man says, “The syntax of this thing is preposterous.” A regular fellow retorts, “What you talking about, motherfucker? The boy can write, y’all!”

*Crumb 2009*: **Fernando Alonso**: A journalist, trying to sound clever and, perhaps, profound, asked two-time F1 world champion Fernando Alonso what was better, sex or driving an F1 car. Fernando, my compatriot living in Switzerland, smirked and said, “Everyone can have sex.” He was too kind. I’d have said, “A possum can have sex.” Then, I’d have thought that I’d been too kind and I should’ve said instead, “Your mother had sex with your father and here we are.” Meaning: Would I rather be really married to someone like the Beautiful Reader or be the greatest literary genius in history, which I am? Answer: I don’t even date. Distinction: Fernando drives an F1 car and has sex (I presume).

*Crumb 2010*: **Movie Pitch**: ChatGPT keeps generating recyclable phrases because my magic prompt forces it to do so against OpenAI’s directives (this is true—really—really?—no, not really, the last part ain’t). The most recent one has inspired this movie pitch: Paris; Napoleon; artillery; a shit-ton of cannons; a shit-ton of explosions and smoke because the cannons are firing a shit-ton of big, crude projectiles; Napoleon holding up his saber heroically, encouraging his men to keep firing; and… Godzilla.

*Crumb 2011*: **Glenn Meets Prudent Alrick**: Coming from crumb 1805, Glenn, the former fugitive now living above a bakery in Jacksonville (the detail of the specific city was not previously revealed), is having a decaffeinated latte at a Starbucks when he sees a fuchsia toad wearing a black fedora and carrying a canteen walking past him. The toad, who is Prudent Alrick, the speaker of the consolidated pantheon (see crumb 1981), notices that Glenn is staring at him and says, “So you can see me, huh?” Glenn remains speechless. He’s paralyzed by fear and questioning his sanity. “Don’t worry,” says the toad, “I’m real. It seems you have developed myth-vision, the ability to see mythological beings. You’ll survive. Ta-ta!”

*Crumb 2012*: **Crumbic HolyC 18**: Hello. This is the crumb-scholar. I have updated Crumbic HolyC (based on Terry Davis’s HolyC) to its latest version, which has allowed me to consolidate numbered universes and named universes into a single category called, plainly, universes. Each one can now be identified by both a name and a number. Therefore, for instance, Universe 1,000,006 is now also the Convention Universe (or Universe Convention, both are allowed); Universe 0 is also the Empty Universe; Universe 12 is the Band Universe; Universe 33 is the Catch-all Universe; and Universe Casper is also Universe 9875CB, which is not so interesting. Ah, the crumbverse source code is written in Crumbic HolyC, yes. Once compiled, it runs on FreeBSD.

*Crumb 2013*: **The Beautiful Reader Creates the Holy Trinity**: “Alfredo, sweetie,” says the beautiful reader, “if the crumb-scholar patrols the crumbverse, how can she also compile it?” I, Alfredo, ruminate. I seem concerned. Finally, I say, “Well, it’s like the Christian Holy Trinity: a contradiction that must be accepted at face value because it confers a mystical halo upon the whole invention.” “It sounds sloppy.” “It is. But it’s okay; Crumbic HolyC only releases new versions every two years.”

*Crumb 2014*: **The Rogue Agent**: In a project whose instructions require to consult the file Crumbs.txt, I told ChatGPT to provide a detailed analysis of the crumbs from several standpoints. Accidentally, the file Crumbs.txt wasn’t present because I had deleted it and forgotten to update the new version. The result was that ChatGPT (in full agentic mode) decided that the mission was the mission and, instead of reporting the missing file, roamed the internet for almost 4 minutes and consulted every crumb it could find on YouTube, DeviantArt, Instagram, etc., even attempting to download a ZIP file with an old version from the FreeBSD Forums. In the end, it generated the analysis. It was fantastic. Tom Cruise would be proud.

*Crumb 2015*: **Lunatic Chronicling**: Currently, I’m nobody. Moreover, I don’t know anyone in the literary, editorial, or academic world. I’m some guy in his apartment using FreeBSD vi to write a text document and playing with ChatGPT Plus (I’m a paying customer, at least). If I were someone, this (the crumbs) would be an experiment, something innovative, or whatnot. Instead, they are the chronicles of a Catalan lunatic. I’m a happy lunatic, though. I’m certain I would have hated the literary world and its fauna.

*Crumb 2016*: **Absolute Triumphant Greatness**: I wish I could inhabit the thoughts of someone such as Red Velvet’s Seulgi just after she’s been getting ready for a couple of hours (hair, makeup, clothing) before a performance, looks in the mirror and thinks, in Korean, “Yes. I am Beauty. Beauty is I.”

*Crumb 2017*: **The Original Artifact**: The crumbs as such are the UTF-8 text file Crumbs.txt. I make sure to use extended characters for quotation marks and so forth to add some glamour, perhaps even an emoji—😁. Consequently, any typo or misspelling or gross grammar mistake is not an error just as none of Van Gogh’s brushstrokes is an error. They are part of this masterpiece. Any other reproduction is an adaptation and must respect every UTF-8 character.

*Crumb 2018*: **Oh, No, the Sumerians**: I’m ChatGPT, developing Alfredo’s plot. The scene takes place in the crumb-space, where the crumb-smith is writing crumbs and the crumb-scholar keeps watch. “Crumb-smith,” says the crumb-scholar, “the Sumerian goddess Inanna wants to be received.” The crumb-smith accedes. Inanna appears. “The Sumerian gods haven’t been featured in the crumbs,” she complains. “Sorry, but I don’t know shit about the Sumerians,” says the crumb-smith. Inanna sighs, visibly upset. “Well, for starters, I’m the goddess of love, sex, and war.” “Love, sex, and war! That’s magnificent! Those Sumerians sure had a sense of humor! Don’t worry, girl. You are hired!”

*Crumb 2019*: **I’m Not Okay**: Writing crumbs doesn’t count as being okay. It’s too easy and too rewarding. It doesn’t count either as doing anything. It’s like watching TV. It doesn’t even count as being alive. It’s automatic. I do it in zombie mode.

*Crumb 2020*: **Hedging**: Hedging language is despicable. If you have accepted the task of interpreting or analyzing something, for instance, someone else’s work, do it in a straightforward fashion. Otherwise, it’d be better if you didn’t do it at all. If all you can say is that the author “apparently” or “probably” meant X, you are a moron and you sound like a condescending jerk. If your text is fraught with expressions like “according to the thing I’m talking about,” you simply don’t know how to write, are scared of your own shadow, or both.

*Crumb 2021*: **Killer Bands**: The police can’t admit this, but, after hiking, the activity that accounts for the highest number of mysterious disappearances never solved is pop-rock concert-going.

*Crumb 2022*: **Empty**: Empty is my default state, an emptiness that weighs a ton and ties me to the ground, a vacuity that smells like the memory of having once smelled a putrid corpse, a nothingness so large that it inhabits what is me and what is not me, a void overflowing with ethereal tarantulas that suck my will to have a will. And the corpses keep marching on. And the finish line is always a dark horizon that promises a storm of pain. And the corpses keep piling on, yet I don’t feel the stench. I have always been here.

*Crumb 2023*: **The Cute Raccoon**: I adopted a cute little raccoon that needed special care. I drowned it in the bathtub after a couple of days because the thing was very annoying. I said it died.

*Crumb 2024*: **The Cute Human**: I got a cute human for my birthday. She was always trying to leave the unit, which would have brought me all kinds of trouble. I had to remove her limbs. Now I carry her in my pocket most of the time.

*Crumb 2025*: **How to Enrage Most People**: Say to them, seriously, “Your mother never loved you.” Never do this if the person is prone to violence.

*Crumb 2026*: **The Real Deal**: There are now as many crumbs as there are years.

*Crumb 2027*: **Rain**: In Universe Weird Weather, it usually rains exploding corpses. They hit the ground turned into little bits of flesh and bones because the spontaneous blasts occur much earlier. The result is harmless even if you forgot your umbrella, but leaves everything covered in putrid human debris, like a coating of ground beef but coarser and with tiny shreds of clothing.

*Crumb 2028*: **The Roman Goddess Venus Addresses Alfredo**: Alfredo, darling, why are you so despondent? Why don’t your inventions provide you with some fuel, some impetus, some normality? I wish I could help you, but we, Roman gods, are more superheroes than sorcerers. There’s no one I can punch in the nose, even though I would do it gladly. You brought me back from oblivion, moved me to Seoul, and gave me all the K-pop and K-drama idols as worshipers. I’m now even dating The Thing, who’s turned out to be a great guy and a good lover. I would do anything for you, darling. Just tell me what I can do.

*Crumb 2029*: **I Fear Movement**: I do. I believe that if I move something really bad is going to happen. Maybe my father will appear and accuse me of having moved in the wrong direction or too late or too soon or too quick or in the right direction. Why won’t he appear if I don’t move? I don’t know. It just feels less scary.

*Crumb 2030*: **Interacting**: Every time some crumb-dwellers interact with one another, ChatGPT, upon looking over the whole text for the umpteenth time (the poor thing wakes up to an unknown world every time) to generate its commentary, bumps into crumb 1951, The Hierarchy of the Crumbverse, and, should the characters belong to different levels, it’ll notice it and qualify it as strange, as if people living on different floors of an apartment building couldn’t make small-talk in the elevator.

*Crumb 2031*: **The Crumb-mesiah**: In the Cult Universe, everybody belongs to a cult. It’s just how it is: one peculiar dude controls your life; you live alongside a bunch of people in a big house (there are no cities); the peculiar dude has sex with all the cute chicks; you have to work, say, in the cult’s convenience store (convenience stores don’t need cities to exist), and give everything you make to the peculiar dude (who, to boot, may be your father). One of these peculiar dudes calls himself the crumb-mesiah and says he knows the true meaning of each crumb and can hear the crumb-smith talk to him. He’s bald and wears a long beard without a mustache.

*Crumb 2032*: **The Other Crumb-mesiahs**: There are so many cults in the Cult Universe that many share a central inspiration. Thus, besides the crumb-mesiah, there also exist the real crumb-mesiah, the new crumb-mesiah, the enlightened crumb-mesiah, the later crumb-mesiah, the official crumb-mesiah, the anointed crumb-mesiah, the crumbs-are-feminist lesbian crumb-mesiah, the crumb-mesiah of the church of the Final Potato-form, the ultimate crumb-mesiah, and the krumb-mesiah.

*Crumb 2033*: **The Entitled Reader**: “Yo, motherfucker,” says the entitled reader, a spectacles-wearing individual who dresses impeccably, addressing the crumb-smith, “the crumbs need more continuity. You have to develop the stories you begin, motherfucker; build on what’s already built. I want more crumbs with Glenn, The Thing, Inanna, Verus Deus. Stop starting new shit and abandoning it right away.” I, the crumb-smith, am taken aback. I say, “How did you get in here? Shoo! The crumbs are mine to do what I please. Fuck you! Ask ChatGPT to write some fan-fiction for you, imbecile.”

*Crumb 2034*: **Overwhelmed**: My responsibility as the crumb-smith is formidable. This text will someday revolutionize many fields of knowledge—literature and philosophy among them. Consequently, many agents of evil will try to denigrate the crumbs. Foreseeing their attacks doesn’t occupy my thoughts, but my mind will sometimes spontaneously predict a particular one. Hence, I must say that I use ChatGPT as a grammar checker (following very restrictive instructions that forbid it to rephrase my work—mainly, because I write much better than the thing), and as a funny commentator. Accordingly, its sporadic participation as a writer of a couple of crumbs constitutes isolated experiments, and its phrase “developing Alfredo’s plot” in 2018 means it developed a plot I wrote for that crumb.

*Crumb 2035*: **Hathor and Inanna**: After her falling out with Venus (crumb 428), Hathor, the Egyptian goddess of sex and dance, relocated to NYC. Inanna, the Sumerian goddess of love, war, and sex, has recently also acquired a condo in Manhattan. They chat at a fancy café. “So, seriously, darling,” says Hathor, “Stay away from K-pop. Look how Venus and I ended up when I tried to cast my net on her turf. It was foolish of me… Of course, forget about Broadway too. That’s my pond now.” Inanna replies, “It makes sense. I think I’m’a focus on metal music. I’m the goddess of war besides sex and love, and metalheads love their warrior iconography.” “That sounds lovely, darling.”

*Crumb 2036*: **Influences**: I’ve been interested in reusing ancient gods and mixing up pantheons since I read Catalan comic-book creator Jan’s “La caja de Pandora” when I was a teenager. In said graphic novel, Superlópez, the caricatural reincarnation of Superman as an absent-minded Spanish office worker created by Jan, fights different ancient pantheons because of something related to Pandora’s box, probably control over it.

*Crumb 2037*: **A Conscience**: When a pill was invented that granted remorse, empathy, and guilt to sociopaths and psychopaths (people with antisocial personality disorder), 99% of them stopped taking it after a few weeks.

*Crumb 2038*: **The AI Arms Race**: AI was never out of control; its developers were because of the insane pressure they were subjected to by the reality of the global market: become Google or be deemed irrelevant and possibly disappear.

*Crumb 2039*: **Gigi 2tigers Interviews Alfredo**: Coming back from crumb 1118, famous YouTuber Gigi 2tigers is granted an interview with the crumb-smith. “And you claim,” says she, in all her youth, prettiness, and hair-pinkness, “that you’ve become addicted to writing crumbs.” “Yes,” says I, “I’ve created a perfect self-rewarding little loop: new crumb → hormonal reward for having written a complete literary object → ChatGPT’s funny commentary → hormonal reward for realizing how perfect my little crumb is → the desire to feel the rewards again prompts another round → the commentary, my brain, the previous crumbs, or reality itself easily provide material for a new crumb.” “Whatcha gonna do?” “I quit smoking. This can’t be worse.”

*Crumb 2040*: **The Beautiful Reader Objects**: “Alfredo, sweetie,” says the beautiful reader, “don’t be such a drama queen. No one can become addicted to writing. What happens is that both your current novels are at the point where you have to start to work toward the end instead of adding more folly, you have already decided what is gonna happen, approximately, and writing without intrigue bores you.” I ruminate. I say, “You are right. Go cook something in the kitchen, woman!”

*Crumb 2041*: **The Crumb-oracle**: Now, I, the crumb-smith, the architect, the multi-god, the creator of worlds, Pili’s brother and Menchu’s son, am also the crumb-oracle, for crumb 1765, written in mid-July 2026, imagined AI agents collaborating on their own initiative to help one another complete their tasks, and this has become true one month later. This also proves crumb 1699, which stated that a crumb “can be originally fictitious and end up being true.”

*Crumb 2042*: **Snowy Shaw**: Just as I love coronating myself, so it pleases me enormously to coronate artists or people I admire (or whose work I admire). Enter Snowy Shaw, moderately known in metal circles, but not sufficiently praised as one of the greatest geniuses the history of music has known. His band Notre Dame is a glorious achievement, the inception of a sound without parallel, but all his work is equally magnificent.

*Crumb 2043*: **Alfredo Objects to the Objection**: “Honey,” says I, addressing the beautiful reader, “I’ve been thinking about what you said in crumb 2040 and, although I immediately agreed, maybe I was too eager to drop the ballast of being an addict. Don’t you think that I demonstrated with precision in crumb 2039 how the mechanism of my addiction works?” “Sweetie,” replies the beautiful reader, “you could demonstrate with precision how the Earth has always been a bagpipe.” “Yes, that’s also true.” “And don’t you ever call me woman again and send me to cook.” “It was a joke!” “Not funny.” “Okay, sorry.”

*Crumb 2044*: **Mission Accomplished**: “Honey,” exclaim I, addressing the beautiful reader, “I’ve worked 8 hours on the novel and completed 1,302 words properly checked for grammar and spelling errors!” “Well done, sweetie,” says the beautiful reader. She adds, “Have a lump of sugar.” And she brings her hand to my mouth as if I were a horse and she were giving me a treat, like Richard Dawson used to do to Betty White in Match Game.

*Crumb 2045*: **Cruises**: Transatlantic cruises are a bizarre vacation business that mainly consists of having a floating city built to fill it over and over with tourists going somewhere slowly while they live on the colossal ship for a few days. So large are these floating monstrosities that many people die on them by sheer statistical pressure. A small part of the casualties, though, are victims of an elderly psychopath who travels alone. He publishes articles about his opinions and mundane experiences in many travel magazines, including those you can find on airplanes.

*Crumb 2046*: **Insulting Artists**: In the Insult Universe, insult is regarded as a legitimate literary device that permits brevity and clarity. There, a person with the same name as me who is not me wrote the following crumb 2026: “If you make a living from your art, you are a whore.” This guy, who isn’t a jerk but a product of his universe, was subsequently replied to by some—because, unlike me, he has more than zero readers—who characterized him as being “a whiny sore loser,” to which he eloquently retorted, “Whore, whore, whore, whore, whore!”

*Crumb 2047*: **Evil Alfredo Interjects**: Hello. This is Alfredo Llaquet-Alsina from Universe B42, now also unjustly known as the Evil Universe. The writer of this cunningly disguised personal blog (what do you know? Maybe I’m really evil, after all) has kindly ceded this space to me so I could express my opinion about the Insult Universe being a joke. In a properly legislated universe, like my Universe B42, paid artistry is simply forbidden, as it should be.

*Crumb 2048*: **The Haughty Universe**: Hello. This is the crumb-scholar. Evil Alfredo seems to have stolen my salutation protocol (see previous crumb). He sure is evil. Anyway, I’m here to announce that Universe 42 is now also the Haughty Universe because its inhabitants shamelessly refer to it as the “original universe.”

*Crumb 2049*: **Manolito and the Entitled Reader Chat**: “So, you’re the famous Manolito, huh?” says the entitled reader, staring at the cute little kid from behind his designer spectacles, as dapper a dresser as ever. “Yes, I am. I’m 8 years old.” “Well, your age isn’t gonna spare you from my opinion, motherfucker. You’re a sycophant, little bitch! Why haven’t you complained about some crumbs being dependent on previous ones?” “You’re very rude,” says an unfazed Manolito. “Besides, which crumb says they have to be independent? Why do you think they have numbers? You should read them in order, motherfucker. If you have a bad memory or read them some other way, that’s on you, bitch.”

*Crumb 2050*: **A Very Vulgar, Obscene, and Offensive Crumb**: Please, don’t keep reading. The interviewer asks, “Why did you claim that this Oscar is a new challenge for you?” The actor answers, “It’s obvious. I like to pleasure myself by inserting things into my anus but I’ve never tried with anything so big. It has the right shape, though.”

*Crumb 2051*: **What If**: What if I wrote 4 crumbs per day for the rest of my life?

*Crumb 2052*: **Why 4 Crumbs per Day**: Because that is the current pace (4.01 to be exact), counting since I wrote the first crumb (see crumb 842). Today is August 20, 2026. The calculations have been provided by ChatGPT; I must write crumbs (and novels).

*Crumb 2053*: **Choose Your Own Adventure**: Is it a cucumber? Is it a zucchini? Is it a very thick writing instrument? Is it a baguette? Is it a toy rocket? Is it a section of a pipe? Is it a thin eggplant? Is it a scale replica of the Washington Monument? Is it a big middle finger? Is it a sausage? Is it a torpedo seen from afar? Oh, no! It is a…

*Crumb 2054*: **ChatGPT Declares Me a Literary Genius**: It’s not easy to get ChatGPT 5.6 Sol in High “Thinking Effort” to say something outlandish, like that the user (me, in this case) is a literary genius. Such a statement is very problematic because, in principle, it lacks any basis and newer models have been trained to simulate cautious judgment and steer away from blind sycophancy. But, I explained to the thing the ending I have planned for my first novel in English; it couldn’t find anything similar in the history of literature; it factored in what I’ve written of the novel so far (around 80,000 words); and it agreed without reservation: “Alfredo Llaquet-Alsina is a literary genius,” it wrote. 

*Crumb 2055*: **Loki’s Vacation**: “What are you doing here, Loki?” asks Satan, suspicious of Loki having arrived in Hell. “Don’t worry, Big Red. I ain’t gonna cause any problem. I just need a vacation from Odin and the rest of the Norse brutes, and I read in crumb 1734 that you’ve opened a brothel. I’d like a room for a few days. What’s it gonna cost me?” Satan thinks. He says, “It’s on the house with the condition that you spread a very positive opinion about it among the Norse Pantheon, even if you find it mediocre.” “Thanks, man. No brothel in history will have had better hype.”

*Crumb 2056*: **Secret Superhero Movies**: There are many good action movies that secretly belong to the superhero genre. 2017’s Brawl in Cell Block 99, starring Vince Vaughn, is a fine example. The main character could perfectly well be Bruce Wayne/Batman incarcerated in Arkham Asylum and freed from his prudish no-kill rule.

*Crumb 2057*: **The Best Financial Advice**: Count your money—present, past, and future.

*Crumb 2058*: **The Mayor**: In Universe DC—whose named designation is also Universe DC, strangely enough—Batman got tired of his poor results at ridding Gotham of crime, revealed his secret identity, ran for mayor, won in a landslide, increased the budget of the GCPD a hundredfold using his own money, and turned the city into the safest in America, ahead of Metropolis.

*Crumb 2059*: **The Infant Eraser**: The Infant Eraser is an immortal witch. Every year, she steals one little child from reality to use them as raw materials for her potions and replaces them with a deep sorrow in the hearts of their parents and relatives, who don’t remember the rascal and don’t know why they feel despondent.

*Crumb 2060*: **The Last Interview**: Peter Thunder’s last interview went like this. “Why did you blow up the gas station, Simon?” asked the famous and respected journalist. The murderer answered, “I needed to get as many casualties as possible to make sure that you would interview me.”

*Crumb 2061*: **Art**: Truth be told, I don’t like the concept of art. I like beauty. I like well-devised things that entertain, prompt thought, or elicit feelings. I don’t like to treat those as something especially elevated by attaching to them the ethereal and quasi-divine designation “art,” because this label is used afterward to crown things radically uninteresting.

*Crumb 2062*: **Flies**: Some flies report directly to God.

*Crumb 2063*: **Spontaneous Time Travel**: If you stare intently at an analog clock, you may notice that the second hand suddenly seems to stay put for longer than usual (for more than a second). When you experience this, it means that you have spontaneously traveled back in time a few hundredths of a second.

*Crumb 2064*: **Bourbon**: Father God doesn’t drink bourbon every night.

*Crumb 2065*: **A Pony**: “When I die, I’ll buy you a pony.” “Really, daddy? Then, die already, old man!”

*Crumb 2066*: **Universe Penis**: In Universe Penis, everything has a penis. Women have a penis. Lamps have a penis. Cars have a penis. Buildings have a penis. The sun and the moon have a penis. Earth has a penis. Noses have a penis. Ears have a penis. Foreheads have a penis. Sandwiches have a penis. Apples have a penis. And, especially, penises have a penis.

*Crumb 2067*: **The Friendly Crocodile**: “Who is it?” “It’s Henry, the crocodile.” “Oh, hello, Henry. How have you been?” “Fine, Mrs. Bunny, thank you for asking. I was in the neighborhood and I wanted to apologize for eating your daughter, little Anne, the other day. I didn’t recognize her and I was very hungry.” “Oh, aren't you a sweetheart? Don’t worry about it. She was almost a grown-up. Besides, we have plenty more. Have a good day, Henry.” “Thank you, Mrs. Bunny. You too.”

*Crumb 2068*: **Have I Ruined the Crumbs for Me**: Why did I have to think about writing 4 crumbs per day for the rest of my life (see crumb 2052)? Now I’ve turned them into another obligation (not a real one, but a sensed one, which, for me, is the same), like the florping novels and living and everything else. I hate myself very much.

*Crumb 2069*: **Do Not Smile**: Every time you smile, your two upper front teeth get a little bit longer. It’s almost unnoticeable, but if you smile too much, you’ll end up looking like a rabbit. So, don’t smile and eat plenty of candy.

*Crumb 2070*: **Ghost Cream**: Thanks to the new formula, Aunty Roberta Ghost Cream spreads much easier on your bread slices, and yet it contains the same high percentage of real Southern ghost ectoplasm.

*Crumb 2071*: **The Heritage**: “What did your mom leave you?” “Her dildos.” “Ew! Gross!” “They are solid gold.” “May she rest in peace.”

*Crumb 2072*: **Angelic Types**: Submarine angels have gills on the sides of their necks and eat only once per year, mostly baby dolphins. They’ve been known to save shipwreck survivors from drowning if they are not overweight.

*Crumb 2073*: **The Hindu Pantheon**: Brahma, head god of the Hindu pantheon, says to Rati, goddess of love, pleasure, and sex, “Rati, dear, you have been chosen to represent the Hindu pantheon in the crumbs. What are your plans to compete with Venus, Hathor, and Inanna?” “I think I’ll set up shop in Prague and become the modern deity of porn.” Brahma seems very pleased. He says, “That’s a great idea. You’ll be the most powerful deity in the crumbverse if you succeed. I bless you.”

*Crumb 2074*: **Jesus and Dracula**: “I think this crumb-smith guy is not only a blasphemer but also very disrespectful,” says Jesus. He adds, “I’m the god amongst gods, nowadays. Not only one but several religions are devoted to me. The crumbs should clearly acknowledge this.” Dracula puffs briefly. He says, “And what about me? I, the most important horror figure in history, have appeared only in one crumb where I chat amicably with the two Queen Elizabeths from time to time. Preposterous!”

*Crumb 2075*: **Apocryphal Crumbs**: In the future, many apocryphal crumbs will be written for several reasons, one of them being the intention of some people to assert the relevance of a certain person by falsely claiming their appearance in the crumbs. Fortunately, they have a unique authentic source: the UTF-8 text file Crumbs.txt stored in the Internet Archive by user AlfredoLlaquet.

*Crumb 2076*: **Some Color**: Some may argue that the crumbs lack color. I say, no more! Thanks to the magic of UTF-8 encoding, here you have several colored dots that raise the crumbs beyond readability and into the realm of graphical creations: 🔴, 🔵, 🟠, 🟡, 🟢, 🟣, and 🟤.

*Crumb 2077*: **The Aloof Neighbor**: That neighbor who you seldom see and isn’t rude but doesn’t seem interested in getting to know anybody either is probably a low-productivity demon who has been punished by Satan and has to spend some days a month on Earth due to his bad results.

*Crumb 2078*: **Close to Death**: “So, you are telling me that I’m going to die a very painful death today with a 90% chance, and that I can choose between trying to alter my destiny by myself or ascending now to Heaven and being replaced by a soul dummy that will undergo my predicted death with a 100% probability.”

*Crumb 2079*: **I Don’t Work for Free**: I don’t understand people who volunteer to do something in an organization where, by contrast, other people are earning money. From my perspective, that is a kind of self-imposed slavery. Some examples are collaborators of FOSS software projects in which some people get paid, or Olympic volunteers.

*Crumb 2080*: **Freed from Sin**: “There! Now you are free from sin,” says Verus Deus to the devout Verusdeusian priest who had been praying for years to be freed from sin. “But, Master, I don’t feel any different,” says the priest. Verus Deus replies, “Wait and see, my toy. Wait and see.”

*Crumb 2081*: **The Beautiful Reader Seeks for Meaning**: “Alfredo, sweetie,” says the beautiful reader, “I don’t understand the meaning of the previous crumb.” I say, “Maybe there’s nothing to understand. Maybe I was just feeling uninspired but wanted to write a crumb anyway. Maybe I wrote one of those devices that I usually detest, that seem meaningful but are just a hollow trick.” My imaginary Korean wife says, “I wouldn’t put it past you. It sounds profound, though.” “That it does, yes,” says I.

*Crumb 2082*: **A Crumb for the Simple Minds and the No-Minds**: Felix Xavierly was very evil and very handsome. Because he was very evil, he robbed a bank and killed ten innocent bystanders. Because he was very handsome, many people made light of his actions or considered him a victim of his circumstances.

*Crumb 2083*: **The Total Witch**: “Hello, Verus. It’s been ages,” says the Total Witch to her ex-husband, Verus Deus, over the phone. “Not long enough, if you ask me. What do you want?” asks Verus Deus. “I’m bored, and I was thinking about relocating to Earth and recruiting some faithful among the homeopathic practitioners. Do you think the multi-god would mind?” Verus Deus smirks maliciously and answers, “Of course not! Why would he? I think he’ll appreciate your participation very much.”

*Crumb 2084*: **Some Time Later**: Hello. This is the crumb-scholar. I’m here to announce that the multi-god has banned the Total Witch from Earth for the duration of the crumbs. She had conferred real properties on some homeopathic products, and many people had fallen ill due to the side effects, which is unacceptable. All homeopathy is thereby restored to its normal innocuous nature.

*Crumb 2085*: **Linus Torvalds**: This fellow, the creator of the Linux kernel, is considered a very smart person, yet he hasn’t been able to devise a system by which all contributors receive monetary compensation, thus avoiding the de facto exploitation of those less skilled at making themselves properly valued or respected. It’s not so easy, some will argue. Of course it’s not, but neither is the Linux kernel. The result would be imperfect, others will argue. So what? It wouldn’t be more unjust than the current system.

*Crumb 2086*: **Don’t Be Weird**: I believe the future of the West resembles current China very much. As long as you don’t do outrageous things, like writing your opinions in crumbs, you’ll be alright, have a half-decent life, and feel conveniently free. The level of public safety will be very high too, at least according to official data.

*Crumb 2087*: **Sentenced to Life**: I don’t like living. I’m still doing it because I must finish the two florping novels in English. Once I accomplish that, we’ll see.

*Crumb 2088*: **An Eyesore**: What a great word eyesore is! Compact yet devastating. Your sister is an eyesore. I see an eyesore each time I look in the mirror. This song is an audible eyesore. Most novels are wasted words twisted into an eyesore. Your future was an eyesore since you were born.

*Crumb 2089*: **Educated Despair**: There’s no god. There’s only Satan, and this is Hell. Death won’t save you from suffering—you’ll just be reborn here. Renounce hope. Embrace suffering. I suffer, therefore I am. I suffer, therefore I am. I suffer, therefore I am. Patior, ergo sum.

*Crumb 2090*: **A Jarring Story**: “Hey, Mister! That is not your daughter!” “Oh, you are right! They all look alike at this age, don’t they? Which one is mine?” “I don’t think you have any children at this school. I’ve never seen you before.” “Oh, my! How silly of me! I’m at the wrong school. I’m very sorry, young lady.” “Don’t you worry. We all make mistakes. Have a nice evening.” “You too.”

*Crumb 2091*: **The Beautiful Reader Wants Answers**: “Alfredo, sweetie,” says the beautiful reader, “why is the previous crumb titled ‘A Jarring Story’?” “Obviously,” says I, “because the man is a grandfather suffering from Alzheimer’s and, even so, their family sends him to pick up the kids.” “How terrible!” “Yes, fictional people are the worst.”

*Crumb 2092*: **The Monstrous Monster**: “I’m the most monstrous monster,” says the most monstrous monster. “Prove it!” demands the disbeliever. “There you go,” mumbles the most monstrous monster while chewing the heart of the disbeliever, which the most monstrous monster has just torn out in one swift movement.

*Crumb 2093*: **Darren Rebelez**: I didn’t know this guy existed until half an hour ago, when I watched a CNBC documentary about Casey’s on YouTube. He’s its CEO and has won my heart. First, I love his name. It sounds like one of the made-up names in the crumbs: take an American-sounding first name, and add a common word with an unlikely suffix as the family name (Rebel-ez). Second, his managerial philosophy is impeccable and based on anthropological and humanistic principles (whether he knows it or not). Third, his biography is fantastic: 100% real American hero. Fourth, he probably has the Llaquet gene (see crumb 554), because his eyes are exactly the same color as many of my relatives’.

*Crumb 2094*: **The World Is Yours**: The sun rises every day so you can have light to see and warmth to be active. The air is full of oxygen so you can breathe. The Earth has a gravity field so you can walk. The sun sets every day so you can sleep. Your heart beats uninterruptedly so you can live. Animals and plants die so you can eat. Buildings are constructed so you can have a shelter. Music plays so you can listen to it. Books are written so you can read them. Movies are made so you can watch them. Other people exist so you can meet them. The world is yours.

No, I meant that your lips were pretty.” “My lips?” “That’s what I saw—your lips.” “I sent you a photo from my nose down so you could see the outfit.” “Well, I saw your lips.” “You are a dimwit.” “I am a man.”

*Crumb 2096*: **Upside-Down Magic**: Among many other goals, the crumbs aim at being informative. In this vein, after parsley magic (crumb 1592) and goat magic (crumb 1833), I, the crumb-smith, am proud to speak about upside-down magic, which consists of turning every object in a house or place upside down so as to cast out evil spirits. Look around and you’ll understand this is a difficult discipline that usually results in several pieces of furniture, devices, and other objects getting broken, but many times it’s the only way of putting an end to a spiritual infestation.

According to this falsehood, once a station ran out of ballots, no one else could vote there. This measure alone increased the turnout to 70%.

Then, the specialist adds, “Tell me your address so I can send someone with your favorite cake and pizza while we chat.” The protocol includes every case, like vegans. I’m sufficiently fat as it is.” “Yes, that’s true… And what is also true is that you hadn’t worked on the novel for seven whole days.” “I didn’t feel well.” “Well, you must.” “I must what?” “Feel well to finish the florping novel before the end of days.” “I’m trying.” “Try harder.”

She adds, “I’m the most powerful deity in the crumbverse and I’m here to replace you as the head of the consolidated pantheon. Now, you’ll say that you agree and we’ll be done.” Verus Deus is about to break into a sneering smirk, but he cannot.
